title: "International reactions to the Gaza war"
---

# International reactions to the Gaza war

For international protests, see [Gaza war protests](75044900.md).

On 7 October 2023, [a large escalation of the Gaza–Israel conflict began](74998519.md) with a [coordinated offensive by multiple Palestinian militant groups](74998317.md), led by [Hamas](13913.md), against [Israel](9282173.md). A number of countries, including many of Israel's Western allies, such as the United States and a number of European countries, condemned the attacks led by Hamas, expressed solidarity for Israel and stated that Israel has a right to defend itself from armed attacks, while countries in the [Islamic world](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islamic_world) (including the [Axis of Resistance](36658251.md)) expressed support for the Palestinians, blaming the [Israeli occupation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israeli_occupation) of the [Palestinian territories](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palestinian_territories) as being the root cause for the escalation of violence. The events prompted several world leaders to announce their intention to visit Israel, including US President [Joe Biden](145422.md),[^cite_note-1][^cite_note-2][^cite_note-3][^cite_note-4] French President [Emmanuel Macron](43671127.md),[^cite_note-5][^cite_note-6][^cite_note-7] German Chancellor [Olaf Scholz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Olaf_Scholz),[^cite_note-8][^cite_note-9][^cite_note-10][^cite_note-independent-diplomaticties-11] and British Prime Minister [Rishi Sunak](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rishi_Sunak).[^cite_note-12][^cite_note-13][^cite_note-14]

Numerous countries [called for a ceasefire](75313349.md) and de-escalation. International organizations, student organizations, charities, ecumenical Christian organizations, and Jewish and Islamic groups commented on the situation. On 27 October 2023, the [United Nations General Assembly](31957.md) passed [a resolution calling for](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Nations_General_Assembly_Resolution_ES-10/21) an immediate and sustained humanitarian truce and cessation of hostilities, adopted by a vote of 121 states to 14, with 44 abstentions.[^cite_note-un-20231027-15][^cite_note-16][^cite_note-17][^cite_note-18] As a result of the war, [Bahrain](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bahrain%E2%80%93Israel_relations),[^cite_note-bhrrecall-19][^cite_note-20] [Chad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chad%E2%80%93Israel_relations),[^cite_note-chdrecall-21][^cite_note-22] [Chile](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chile%E2%80%93Israel_relations),[^cite_note-chirecall-23][^cite_note-24] [Honduras](13394.md),[^cite_note-honrecall-25] [Jordan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israel%E2%80%93Jordan_relations),[^cite_note-jorrecall-26] [South Africa](6699387.md),[^cite_note-zafrecall-27] and [Turkey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israel%E2%80%93Turkey_relations), recalled their ambassadors from Israel, citing Israeli actions during the war,[^cite_note-turrecall-28] while [Belize](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Belize),[^cite_note-belize-29] [Bolivia](58066729.md),[^cite_note-ReutersBolivia2-30] [Colombia](15647808.md),[^cite_note-31][^cite_note-Cobb-32] and [Nicaragua](21362.md) severed diplomatic relations with Israel.[^cite_note-33]

## Countries

|  | This section needs to be **updated**. The reason given is: **Most of the reactions are only about the initial Hamas attack**. Please help update this article to reflect recent events or newly available information. *(January 2024)* |
| --- | --- |



On 27 October, the [United Nations General Assembly](31957.md) passed [Resolution ES-10/21](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Nations_General_Assembly_Resolution_ES-10/21) calling for an "immediate and sustained" humanitarian truce and condemned "all acts of violence aimed at Palestinian and Israeli civilians".



On 12 December, the UN General Assembly passed [Resolution ES-10/22](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Nations_General_Assembly_Resolution_ES-10/22) calling for an immediate ceasefire and the "immediate and unconditional" release of the hostages.

### Africa

-  [Algeria](358.md): The Foreign Ministry issued a statement heavily condemning the Israeli bombing of Gaza, urging international bodies to intervene in the conflict and declaring sympathy for the Palestinians fighting against "Israeli colonial settlement".

  [^cite_note-34]

  &#x20;In March 2024, Algeria proposed a motion to the [United Nations Security Council](31956.md) to release a statement condemning the [Flour massacre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Flour_massacre), which was blocked by the United States.

  [^cite_note-35]

-  [Angola](701.md): Angola called for restrain and dialogue by both parties.

  [^cite_note-auto5-36]

-  [Benin](3459.md): In December 2023, Benin voted against condemning Hamas at the [United Nations](31769.md).

  [^cite_note-twitter.com-37]

-  [Botswana](3464.md): The Foreign Ministry released a statement condemning violence against civilians by both sides and urged for an immediate cessation of hostilities.

  [^cite_note-38]

-  [Burundi](21490998.md): In December 2023, Burundi voted against condemning Hamas at the United Nations.

  [^cite_note-twitter.com-37]

-  [Cape Verde](18962637.md): Cape Verde voted to condemn Hamas at the United Nations.

  [^cite_note-auto4-39]

-  [Cameroon](5447.md): Cameroon backed Israel and condemned Hamas.

  [^cite_note-globeecho.com-40]

-  [Central African Republic](5478.md): The Central African Republic voted against condemning Hamas at the United Nations.

  [^cite_note-auto4-39]

-  [Chad](5488.md): Chad voted against condemning Hamas at the United Nations.

  [^cite_note-auto4-39]

  &#x20;On 5 November, it recalled its ambassador to Israel.

  [^cite_note-chdrecall-21]

-  [Comoros](5403.md): Comoros voted against condemning Hamas at the United Nations.

  [^cite_note-auto4-39]

-  [Republic of the Congo](19599929.md): RO Congo voted against condemning Hamas at the United Nations.

  [^cite_note-auto4-39]

-  [Democratic Republic of the Congo](76762.md): The DRC condemned Hamas and backed Israel.

  [^cite_note-41]

-  [Djibouti](17207794.md): Djibouti expressed support for Palestine.

  [^cite_note-African_reactions_Oct9-42]

-  [Egypt](8087628.md): Egypt urged both Israel and Palestine to exercise restraint.

  [^cite_note-voa-43]

  &#x20;It also said the international community should "urge Israel to stop the attacks and provocative actions against the Palestinian people and to adhere to the principles of international humanitarian law with regard to the responsibilities of an occupying state."

  [^cite_note-voa-43]

  &#x20;The Foreign Ministry warned of "severe dangers" and encouraged de-escalation.

  [^cite_note-44]

  &#x20;On 25 October, Egyptian President [Abdel Fattah el-Sisi](36703624.md) warned that a [ground invasion of the Gaza Strip](74998519.md) would cause "many, many civilian casualties".

  [^cite_note-france24-invasion-45]

  &#x20;Sisi said that Israel's bombing of Gaza "went beyond the right to self-defense, turning into [collective punishment](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Collective_punishment) for 2.3 million people in Gaza."

  [^cite_note-46]

-  [Eritrea](17238590.md): In December 2023, Eritrea voted against condemning Hamas at the United Nations.

  [^cite_note-twitter.com-37]

-  [Eswatini](27451.md): The government said that they would not take sides in the Israeli–Palestinian conflict.

  [^cite_note-47]

-  [Gabon](12027.md): Gabon was one of four countries to vote at the United Nations Security Council in favor of a Russian-drafted resolution that condemned all violence in Gaza without explicitly condemning Hamas.

  [^cite_note-apnews.com-48]

-  [Gambia](65656.md): The Gambia voted against condemning Hamas at the United Nations.

  [^cite_note-auto4-39]

-  [Ghana](12067.md): The Foreign Ministry released a statement saying that "While Ghana affirms its support for Israel's right to exist and defend itself, it calls on the Israeli government to exercise restraint in its response to Hamas attacks".

  [^cite_note-49]

-  [Guinea](12176.md): Guinea voted against condemning Hamas at the United Nations.

  [^cite_note-auto4-39]



Guinea-Bissau's President [Umaro Sissoco Embaló](52335811.md) and Israeli President [Isaac Herzog](4645588.md) in Jerusalem, 3 March 2024

-  [Guinea-Bissau](12186.md): President [Umaro Sissoco Embaló](52335811.md) condemned the actions by Israel when he called for "a cease-fire in the Gaza Strip, lamenting the escalation of violence and loss of human life."

  [^cite_note-auto5-36]

  &#x20;He later called on "both sides to hold back because there is a lot of human loss."

-  [Kenya](188171.md): [Korir Sing'Oei](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Korir_Sing'Oei?action=edit\&redlink=1), the [Principal Secretary in the State Department for Foreign Affairs](19958321.md), said that the country strongly condemned "the despicable terror attack against Israel and regrets the carnage and senseless loss of life", while repudiating "the planners, funders and implementers of this heinous attack". It acknowledged Israel's "right to retaliate", but urged "a peaceful path to resolving this unfortunate development".

  [^cite_note-50]

-  [Libya](17633.md): The [High Council of State](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/High_Council_of_State_\(Libya\)) expressed support to Palestine and asked the [Government of National Unity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Government_of_National_Unity_\(Libya\)) to sever diplomatic relationships with countries supporting Israel, while the [Minister of Foreign Affairs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Minister_of_Foreign_Affairs_\(Libya\)) Taher al-Badour called for an immediate halt of Israeli attacks in Gaza.

  [^cite_note-51]

  &#x20;The [House of Representatives](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/House_of_Representatives_\(Libya\)) expressed support for Gaza, condemned Israel and asked the rival [Government of National Stability](71621370.md) to totally halt oil exports to countries which support Israel and the immediate expulsion of the ambassadors of the [United States](3434750.md), the [United Kingdom](31717.md), [France](5843419.md), [Germany](11867.md), and [Italy](14532.md) from Libya.

  [^cite_note-52]

  [^cite_note-53]

-  [Malawi](19088.md): Malawi voted to condemn Hamas at the United Nations.

  [^cite_note-auto4-39]

-  [Mali](19127.md): Mali stated its support for Palestine.

  [^cite_note-globeecho.com-40]

-  [Mauritania](18778516.md): Mauritania condemned Israel's military actions and a call for international intervention to protect Palestinian civilians. The Mauritanian government declared three days of national mourning following a deadly strike on a hospital in Gaza, which they described as "genocide" and urged the international community to impose an immediate halt to the violence.

  [^cite_note-54]

-  [Morocco](19291.md): The Foreign Ministry expressed "its deep concern at the deterioration of the situation and the outbreak of military action in the Gaza Strip", and condemned "attacks against civilians wherever they may be".

  [^cite_note-\:2-55]

  &#x20;Morocco voted against condemning Hamas at the United Nations.

  [^cite_note-auto4-39]

-  [Mozambique](19301.md): Mozambique was one of four countries at the United Nations Security Council that voted in favor of a Russian-drafted resolution that condemned all violence in Gaza without explicitly condemning [Hamas](13913.md).

  [^cite_note-apnews.com-48]

-  [Namibia](21292.md): The government condemned the escalation of violence, urged for diplomatic solutions and called on Israel to "cease its attacks and refrain from engaging in provocative acts against the Palestinian people."

  [^cite_note-56]

-  [Niger](21373.md): Niger stated its support for Palestine.

  [^cite_note-globeecho.com-40]

-  [Nigeria](21383.md): Foreign Minister [Yusuf Tuggar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yusuf_Tuggar) called for de-escalation, and asked both sides "to exercise restraint, prioritise the safety of civilians and give room for humanitarian considerations".

  [^cite_note-57]

-  [Rwanda](25645.md): The government said it "extends its deepest condolences and sympathy to the Government and the People of the State of Israel following attacks on Israeli territory".

  [^cite_note-iol.co.za-58]

-  [Sahrawi Republic](38762388.md): The [Polisario Front](201574.md) expressed support for the Palestinian people.

  [^cite_note-59]

-  [Senegal](27278.md): Senegal voted against condemning Hamas at the United Nations.

  [^cite_note-auto4-39]

-  [Seychelles](27288.md): The Seychelles voted to condemn Hamas at the United Nations.

  [^cite_note-auto4-39]

-  [Somalia](27358.md): Somalia blamed Israel for the [al-Ahli Arab Hospital bombing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al-Ahli_Arab_Hospital_explosion).

  [^cite_note-60]

-  [South Africa](17416221.md): The Foreign Ministry issued a statement calling for de-escalation and blaming the conflagration on "the continued [illegal occupation of Palestine land](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israeli-occupied_Territories), continued [settlement expansion](15123.md), [desecration of the Al-Aqsa Mosque](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_Al-Aqsa_clashes) and Christian holy sites, and ongoing oppression of the Palestinian people". South Africa affirmed its support of a [two-state solution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Two-state_solution).

  [^cite_note-61]

  &#x20;Foreign minister [Naledi Pandor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Naledi_Pandor) was criticized by the South African Jewish Board of deputies for taking a phone call from Hamas ten days after its attack and accused her of taking sides in the conflict. Pandor denied expressing support for Hamas and said that she expressed support for the Palestinian people and discussed aid for the people of Gaza.

  [^cite_note-62]

  [^cite_note-63]

  &#x20;South Africa voted against condemning Hamas at the [United Nations](31769.md).

  [^cite_note-auto4-39]

  &#x20;On 6 November, Minister in the Presidency, [Khumbudzo Ntshavheni](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Khumbudzo_Ntshavheni), announced a withdrawal of all its diplomats from Israel.

  [^cite_note-zafrecall-27]

  &#x20;On 21 November, the [National Assembly](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Assembly_of_South_Africa) passed a motion calling for the closure of the Israeli embassy and the cutting of diplomatic ties until Israel agreed to a ceasefire in Gaza.

  [^cite_note-64]

  &#x20;On 29 December, South Africa submitted the case *[South Africa v. Israel (Genocide Convention)](75736183.md)* to the [International Court of Justice](14918.md), arguing that Israel was responsible for [a genocide of Palestinians in Gaza](75668427.md).

  [^cite_note-65]

  &#x20;In May 2024, ICJ judges called on Israel to halt its intended [military offensive in the city of Rafah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rafah_offensive).

  [^cite_note-66]

-  [South Sudan](32350676.md): President [Salva Kiir Mayardit](2363203.md) wrote to Israeli Prime Minister Benjamin Netanyahu that South Sudan expressed "deepest sympathies to you and the people of Israel for the people you have lost to the heinous and provoked terror by Hamas," stating further that "no amount of political grievances can justify the killings of innocent civilians".

  [^cite_note-67]

  &#x20;South Sudan voted to condemn Hamas at the United Nations.

  [^cite_note-auto4-39]

-  [Sudan](27421.md): The [Foreign Ministry](18387433.md) said that Sudan supported "the legitimate rights of the Palestinian people to have their independent state", and called "for adherence to international resolutions and the protection of innocent civilians".

  [^cite_note-68]

  [^cite_note-African_reactions_Oct9-42]

-  [Tanzania](30118.md): The Foreign Ministry condemned all violence and called for dialogue between both sides.

  [^cite_note-69]

-  [Togo](30138.md): Foreign Minister [Robert Dussey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Robert_Dussey) issued a statement saying that Togo "strongly condemns the Hamas terrorist attack against Israeli civilians."

  [^cite_note-africafr-70]

-  [Tunisia](30188.md): A statement from the Presidency said that the abstention was because the resolution did not go far enough in order to support the Palestinian people. It said that its "position will be to fully stand by the Palestinian people until they recover their entire land".

  [^cite_note-71]

-  [Uganda](31816.md): President [Yoweri Museveni](293409.md) expressed concern for the situation and called for dialogue.

  [^cite_note-72]

-  [Zambia](34415.md): Foreign Minister [Stanley Kakubo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stanley_Kakubo) backed Israel in a statement condemning Hamas' attacks.

  [^cite_note-African_reactions_Oct9-42]

-  [Zimbabwe](34399.md): Zimbabwe condemned Israeli actions in Gaza.

  [^cite_note-iol.co.za-58]

### Americas

See also: [United States support for Israel in the Gaza war](65284610.md)

-  [Antigua and Barbuda](951.md): The government issued a statement condemning Hamas and conveying sympathies to the families of the victims of the attacks.

  [^cite_note-CaribbeanReactions-73]



Argentina's President [Javier Milei](56599840.md) and Israeli President Herzog shake hands in [Beit HaNassi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beit_HaNassi) in Jerusalem on 6 February 2024

-  [Argentina](18951905.md): President [Alberto Fernández](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alberto_Fern%C3%A1ndez) condemned the "brutal terrorist attacks" against Israel,

  [^cite_note-74]

  &#x20;while the [Foreign Ministry](18478549.md) stated that it "deplores" the "terrorist actions of Hamas against Israeli territory", further expressing solidarity with the Israeli people and sending condolences to those affected.

  [^cite_note-75]

  &#x20;Fernández later said on social media that he had made a phone call to Israeli president [Isaac Herzog](4645588.md) during which he expressed Argentina's "energic repudiation and condemnation of the atrocious terrorist attacks committed by Hamas."

  [^cite_note-76]

  &#x20;After the change of government in December 2023, newly inaugurated president [Javier Milei](56599840.md) stressed his support for Israel during a meeting with Israeli Foreign Minister [Eli Cohen](46195208.md). Argentina also changed its position before the UN to abstaining in a resolution demanding a ceasefire.

  [^cite_note-77]

  [^cite_note-78]

  &#x20;On 14 January 2024, Milei said that Argentina "endorses Israel's right to self-defense."

  [^cite_note-79]

-  [Bahamas](3451.md): Foreign Minister [Fred Mitchell](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fred_Mitchell_\(Bahamian_politician\)) called for the "cessation of activities between Hamas and Israel."

  [^cite_note-80]

-  [Barbados](3455.md): Barbados voted to condemn Hamas at the [United Nations](31769.md).

  [^cite_note-auto4-39]

-  [Belize](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Belize): Foreign Minister [Eamon Courtenay](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eamon_Courtenay) called for de-escalation and urged for Palestinian statehood, with East Jerusalem as the capital.

  [^cite_note-CaribbeanReactions-73]

  &#x20;On 15 November 2023, Belize terminated diplomatic ties with Israel, citing its "indiscriminate bombing" in Gaza.

  [^cite_note-81]

-  [Bolivia](3462.md): The government expressed "deep concern" at the situation and criticized the "inaction of the United Nations and the Security Council."

  [^cite_note-bolpanama-82]

  &#x20;Bolivia voted against condemning Hamas at the [United Nations](31769.md).

  [^cite_note-auto4-39]

  &#x20;On 31 October 2023, the nation cut ties with Israel due to "crimes against humanity".

  [^cite_note-ReutersBolivia2-30]

-  [Brazil](3383.md): The [Foreign Ministry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_Foreign_Affairs_\(Brazil\)) stated that as president of the [United Nations Security Council](31956.md), Brazil would call an emergency meeting of the body and seek to de-escalate the situation.

  [^cite_note-reuters-83]

  &#x20;Brazil condemned the attacks on Israel and expressed condolences with the Israeli people.

  [^cite_note-84]

  &#x20;Brazil also reiterated its support of a [two-state solution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Two-state_solution) based on mutually agreed upon borders.

  [^cite_note-reuters-83]

  &#x20;President [Luiz Inácio Lula da Silva](99328.md) criticized Israel's actions in the Gaza Strip

  [^cite_note-85]

  &#x20;calling it "the insanity" of Prime Minister Benjamin Netanyahu.

  [^cite_note-86]

-  [Canada](5042916.md): Prime Minister [Justin Trudeau](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Justin_Trudeau) said that "Canada strongly condemns the current terrorist attacks against Israel" and called the violence "completely unacceptable". He added that the country stood with Israel and fully supported "its right to defend itself".

  [^cite_note-CTV_Lee_7_October-87]

  &#x20;Trudeau later put out a statement asserting "Hamas does not represent the Palestinian people nor their legitimate aspirations" and "Canada stands firmly with the Israeli and Palestinian peoples in their right to live in peace, security, with dignity and without fear."

  [^cite_note-88]

  &#x20;On 14 November 2023, Trudeau urged Israel to stop "this killing of women, of children, of babies" in the Gaza Strip. His statements were subsequently criticized by Israeli Prime Minister [Benjamin Netanyahu](56472.md).

  [^cite_note-89]

  &#x20;On 21 December, Trudeau stated Israel's actions were putting long-term support into question.

  [^cite_note-90]

-  [Chile](5489.md): The government of President [Gabriel Boric](47329497.md) expressed its "absolute condemnation" of the attacks "against a series of towns and cities in Israel", sending condolences to the victims' families and its solidarity with the people of Israel. It also called for an end to "unconducive" violence, and asked the international community for a "direct and good-faith negotiations" between Israel and Palestine, that could lead to a peace agreement that is "fair, full and definitive". It also supported a two-state solution, with safe frontiers agreed upon mutually and internationally recognized.

  [^cite_note-91]

  &#x20;On 1 November, Chile recalled its ambassador to Israel, citing "unacceptable" violations of international humanitarian law in the Gaza Strip.

  [^cite_note-chirecall-23]

  &#x20;On 18 January 2024, [Foreign Minister](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_Foreign_Affairs_\(Chile\)) [Alberto van Klaveren](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alberto_van_Klaveren) stated the country's interest in an investigation by the [International Criminal Court](14880.md) into Israeli war crimes.

  [^cite_note-M0-92]

-  [Colombia](5222.md): The Foreign Ministry issued a statement calling for an urgent resumption of "dialogue between Israel and Palestine to begin a peace process that leads to peaceful coexistence, within mutually agreed upon secure borders and internationally recognized, with full support for the territorial integrity of the parties."

  [^cite_note-93]

  &#x20;Israel later halted all security exports to Colombia after president [Gustavo Petro](13391962.md) compared the IDF to [Nazi Germany](21212.md).

  [^cite_note-94]

  &#x20;In response, Petro threatened to suspend [diplomatic relations](15647808.md) between the two countries, stating that "we do not support genocides".

  [^cite_note-95]

  &#x20;On 31 October, Petro recalled Colombia's ambassador to Israel in protest over the "massacre of Palestinian people".

  [^cite_note-colrecall-96]

  &#x20;On 1 May 2024, Petro announced that Colombia would sever diplomatic relations with Israel over its conduct in Gaza.

-  [Costa Rica](5551.md): The Foreign Ministry condemned the attacks as terrorism, and expressed its solidarity with Israel.

  [^cite_note-97]

-  [Cuba](5042481.md): President [Miguel Diaz-Canel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Miguel_Diaz-Canel), Prime Minister [Manuel Marrero](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Manuel_Marrero), and Foreign Minister [Bruno Rodríguez Parrilla](15731273.md) joined a pro-Palestine demonstration in [Havana](49719.md) on 23 November.

  [^cite_note-98]

-  [Dominica](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dominica): Prime Minister [Roosevelt Skerrit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roosevelt_Skerrit) condemned the conflict, calling it "abhorrent".

  [^cite_note-CaribbeanReactions-73]

  &#x20;In December 2023, Dominica voted against condemning Hamas at the United Nations.

  [^cite_note-twitter.com-37]

-  [Dominican Republic](8060.md): The Dominican Republic voted to condemn Hamas at the [United Nations](31769.md).

  

  Ecuadorian President [Daniel Noboa](73980059.md) and Israeli Prime Minister [Benjamin Netanyahu](56472.md) during Noboa's state visit to Israel in May 2025

-  [Ecuador](9334.md): The government condemned the attacks on Israel, expressed solidarity with the Israeli people, and reiterated that it was committed to a negotiated, definitive and fair solution for both Israel and Palestine.

  [^cite_note-99]

-  [El Salvador](9356.md): President [Nayib Bukele](46452145.md), who is of Palestinian descent, described Hamas as "savage beasts" on X, stating that Hamas "\[does] not represent the Palestinians" and compared the group to [MS-13](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MS-13), a Salvadoran criminal gang. Bukele expressed his support for the Palestinian people and called on Palestinians to "get rid of those animals \[Hamas] and let the good people thrive".

  [^cite_note-100]

  &#x20;In December 2023, El Salvador voted against condemning Hamas at the United Nations.

  [^cite_note-twitter.com-37]

-  [Guatemala](17238567.md): The Foreign Ministry strongly condemned the attacks. It conveyed its solidarity to the People and Government of Israel, and advocated for the prompt restoration of peace.

  [^cite_note-101]

-  [Guyana](17238691.md): Guyana voted against condemning Hamas at the [United Nations](31769.md).

  [^cite_note-auto4-39]

-  [Honduras](13394.md): The government issued a statement condemning Hamas for the attacks, expressed solidarity with the people of Israel and made a call for a ceasefire.

  [^cite_note-102]

  &#x20;However, on 3 November, Honduras recalled its ambassador to Israel citing violations of international humanitarian law in Gaza.

  [^cite_note-honrecall-25]

-  [Jamaica](15660.md): Prime Minister [Andrew Holness](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Andrew_Holness) condemned the attacks on Israel and conveyed his sympathies to its people.

  [^cite_note-CaribbeanReactions-73]



Pro-Palestinian protest in [Mexico City](18987.md) on 29 November 2023

-  [Mexico](3966054.md): The [Foreign Ministry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Secretariat_of_Foreign_Affairs_\(Mexico\)) condemned the attacks on Israel, and noted that Israel and Palestine had a right to coexist in peace.

  [^cite_note-103]

  [^cite_note-104]

  &#x20;President [Andrés Manuel López Obrador](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Andr%C3%A9s_Manuel_L%C3%B3pez_Obrador) reiterated such on 9 October, calling for a two-state solution.

  [^cite_note-105]

  &#x20;On 18 January 2024, Mexico announced support for investigation into Israeli war crimes in Gaza by the International Criminal Court after reviewing UN reports and the [South Africa's genocide case against Israel](75736183.md).

  [^cite_note-M0-92]

-  [Nicaragua](21362.md): President [Daniel Ortega](8778.md) condemned the "terrible aggravation" of the conflict and said that the country was always in "solidarity with the Palestinian cause."

  [^cite_note-106]

-  [Panama](22997.md): President [Laurentino Cortizo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Laurentino_Cortizo) "firmly" condemned the attacks and expressed solidarity with the Israeli people.

  [^cite_note-bolpanama-82]

-  [Paraguay](23420.md): President [Santiago Peña](58414156.md) condemned "these inhuman actions that threaten the peace of the world", while the Foreign Ministry condemned the attacks.

  [^cite_note-107]

-  [Peru](170691.md): The [Foreign Ministry](17829557.md) strongly condemned the attacks by Hamas, and reaffirmed its commitment to the peace process between Israel and Palestine.

  [^cite_note-108]

-  [Saint Lucia](27208.md): In December 2023, Saint Lucia voted against condemning Hamas at the United Nations.

  [^cite_note-twitter.com-37]

-  [Saint Vincent and the Grenadines](27228.md): Prime Minister [Ralph Gonsalves](393744.md) called for de-escalation and peace.

  [^cite_note-109]

  &#x20;In December 2023, Saint Vincent and the Grenadines voted against condemning Hamas at the United Nations.

  [^cite_note-twitter.com-37]

-  [Suriname](26828.md): Suriname condemned the actions by [Hamas](13913.md) and called for de-escalation.

  [^cite_note-110]

-  [Trinidad and Tobago](3565457.md): In December 2023, Trinidad and Tobago voted against condemning Hamas at the United Nations.

  [^cite_note-twitter.com-37]



U.S. Secretary of State [Antony Blinken](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Antony_Blinken) and Israeli President [Isaac Herzog](4645588.md) in Tel Aviv, Israel, 12 October 2023



U.S. President [Joe Biden](145422.md) with Israeli Prime Minister [Benjamin Netanyahu](56472.md) and President [Isaac Herzog](4645588.md), 18 October 2023



U.S. President [Donald Trump](4848272.md) and Israeli Prime Minister Netanyahu at the White House, 7 April 2025

-  [United States](3434750.md): President [Joe Biden](145422.md) issued a statement condemning the attacks and saying that he was ready to offer "all appropriate means of support to the Government and people of Israel".

  [^cite_note-111]

  [^cite_note-\:4-112]

  &#x20;Acting [ambassador to Israel](8944831.md), [Stephanie Hallett](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stephanie_Hallett) said she was "sickened by the images coming out of southern Israel of dead and wounded civilians at the hands of terrorists from Gaza".

  [^cite_note-113]

  [^cite_note-114]

  &#x20;The White House issued a statement saying the United States "unequivocally condemns the unprovoked attacks by Hamas terrorists against Israeli civilians".

  [^cite_note-115]

  &#x20;Immediately following the initial attacks on Israel, the [US Office of Palestinian Affairs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palestine%E2%80%93United_States_relations) on 7 October urged "all sides" to refrain from "retaliatory attacks".

  [^cite_note-116]

  &#x20;\
  &#x20;US Defense Secretary [Lloyd Austin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lloyd_Austin) on 8 October ordered the deployment of a [carrier strike group](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carrier_strike_group) led by the aircraft carrier [USS *Gerald R. Ford*](4050542.md) to the [Eastern Mediterranean](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eastern_Mediterranean). The group also included the cruiser [USS *Normandy*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/USS_Normandy) and the destroyers [USS *Thomas Hudner*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/USS_Thomas_Hudner), [USS *Ramage*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/USS_Ramage), [USS *Carney*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/USS_Carney), and [USS *Roosevelt*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/USS_Roosevelt_\(DDG-80\)).

  [^cite_note-117]

  &#x20;On 9 October, the [White House](33057.md) was lit up in the colors of the Israeli flag.

  [^cite_note-118]

  &#x20;On 10 October, President Biden announced that "Hamas has set a goal of killing Jews".

  [^cite_note-119]

  [^cite_note-October_10_Biden-120]

  &#x20;He also said that Hamas' attacks aimed to halt an agreement between Israel and Saudi Arabia.

  [^cite_note-121]

  &#x20;The [US Treasury Department](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/US_Treasury_Department) imposed sanctions on ten individuals and entities affiliated with Hamas and its financial networks in retaliation to its attack on Israel.

  [^cite_note-122]

  &#x20;In an interview that aired on 15 October, President Joe Biden said an Israeli occupation of Gaza "would be a big mistake", adding that he was "confident Israel will act under the rules of war".

  [^cite_note-123]

  [^cite_note-124]

  &#x20;He also said that Hamas must be eliminated, that there must be a path to a Palestinian state,

  [^cite_note-auto6-125]

  &#x20;and regarded the initial Hamas attack as consequential as [The Holocaust](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Holocaust).

  [^cite_note-126]

  &#x20;On 12 December, Biden warned that Israel was losing international support because of its "indiscriminate bombing" of Gaza.

  [^cite_note-127]

  &#x20;\
  &#x20;On 1 February 2024, the [US House of Representatives](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/US_House_of_Representatives) passed a bill to prohibit individuals associated with Hamas and other perpetrators of the 7 October attack from seeking immigration-related relief or protections in the United States. The resolution, an amendment to the US immigration code, specifically targets Hamas, Palestinian Islamic Jihad, and the Palestine Liberation Organization. The bill now awaits consideration by the US Senate.

  [^cite_note-128]

  [^cite_note-129]

  &#x20;On 8 February, Biden called Israel's actions in Gaza "over the top".

  [^cite_note-130]

  &#x20;Following this, the Biden administration issued a [national security directive](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_security_directive) requiring written assurances from Israel (and other countries) that it was using US-supplied weapons in line with international law.

  [^cite_note-131]

  [^cite_note-132]

  &#x20;On 20 February, the US proposed a draft UN Security Council resolution, calling for a "temporary ceasefire in Gaza as soon as practicable, based on the formula of all hostages being released". It stated that a [Rafah offensive](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rafah_offensive) would have "serious implications" and "should not proceed under current circumstances". The US said it would not yet put the resolution to a vote as it wanted to allow negotiations to continue.

  [^cite_note-133]

  [^cite_note-134]

  &#x20;In early March, the US began [airdrops](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Airdrops) of aid into Gaza

  [^cite_note-135]

  &#x20;and began building the [Gaza floating pier](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gaza_floating_pier) so aid can be delivered by sea. On 4 March, US vice president [Kamala Harris](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kamala_Harris) called for "an immediate ceasefire for at least the next six weeks" because of "the immense scale of suffering in Gaza". She said Israel must let more aid into Gaza and called on Hamas to accept a ceasefire deal involving the release of hostages.

  [^cite_note-136]

  &#x20;Over 100 American localities had passed [resolutions calling for a ceasefire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Municipal_resolutions_for_a_ceasefire_in_the_Israel%E2%80%93Hamas_war) in the war.

  [^cite_note-137]

  &#x20;On 22 March, the US put forward a draft UN Security Council resolution which stated the "imperative" for "an immediate and sustained ceasefire", facilitating aid delivery and supporting ongoing talks between Israel and Hamas, linked to the release of hostages. It was vetoed by Russia and China.

  [^cite_note-138]

  &#x20;On 25 March, the US abstained on a [UN Security Council ceasefire resolution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Nations_Security_Council_Resolution_2728), allowing it to pass.

  [^cite_note-139]

-  [Uruguay](31843.md): President [Luis Lacalle Pou](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luis_Lacalle_Pou) said he strongly condemned the Hamas attacks and called for an "immediate cessation of violence against the Israeli people".

  [^cite_note-140]

  [^cite_note-141]

  &#x20;The [Foreign Ministry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_Foreign_Relations_\(Uruguay\)) issued a statement in which it expressed that the Government and the Uruguayan people "deplore with the greatest firmness the terrorist actions underway against Israel and its population," and stressed their rejection of terrorism and their commitment to the security of Israel.

  [^cite_note-142]

  [^cite_note-143]

-  [Venezuela](32374.md): The Foreign Ministry issued a statement expressing its "profound concern over the evolution of recent events in the Gaza Strip, while considering that the escalation is the result of the impossibility of the Palestinian people to find a space in multilateral international legality to assert their historical rights". The statement called for dialogue and the implementation of the [United Nations Security Council Resolution 2334](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Nations_Security_Council_Resolution_2334).

  [^cite_note-144]

### Asia



Celebrations in Iran, 7 October 2023

-  [Afghanistan](737.md): The Foreign Ministry said that the country had "carefully monitored the recent events in the Gaza Strip" and considered "the occurrence of such events to be the result of Israeli Zionists trampling on the rights of the oppressed Palestinian people and repeated insults and disrespect to Muslim holy places, and any type of defense and the resistance of the Palestinian people for freedom."

  [^cite_note-145]

-  [Bahrain](18933277.md): The Foreign Ministry called for de-escalation, and affirmed its support of a [two-state solution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Two-state_solution).

  [^cite_note-146]

  &#x20;On 9 October it officially condemned Hamas' kidnappings.

  [^cite_note-147]

  [^cite_note-148]

  &#x20;On 2 November, Bahrain recalled its ambassador to Israel and cut off all economic relations citing its war in Gaza.

  [^cite_note-bhrrecall-19]



Anti-Israel banner at a demonstration in Bangladesh

-  [Bangladesh](3454.md): The [Foreign Ministry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_Foreign_Affairs_\(Bangladesh\)) issued a press statement calling for an immediate ceasefire and solving the conflict with dialogue and diplomacy. It condemned the armed conflict, expressed deep concern over the "tragic loss of civilian lives", and urged both sides to "exercise maximum refrainment".

  [^cite_note-149]

  [^cite_note-150]

  &#x20;It also denounced the "[Israeli occupation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israeli_occupation) and [forced settlements](15123.md) on Palestinian Territories" and reaffirmed its support for the [two-state solution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Two-state_solution).

  [^cite_note-151]

  [^cite_note-152]

  [^cite_note-153]

-  [Brunei](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brunei): Brunei reiterated solidarity with Palestinians.

  [^cite_note-154]

-  [Cambodia](334751.md): The [Foreign Ministry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_Foreign_Affairs_and_International_Cooperation_\(Cambodia\)) issued a statement condemning all forms of violence and terrorist acts and called on all parties involved in the fighting in Israel to exercise the utmost restraint and seek avenues.

  [^cite_note-155]

-  [China](5405.md): The Foreign Ministry expressed concern over the "escalation of tensions and violence between Palestine and Israel", urging relevant parties to "exercise restraint and immediately end the hostilities to protect civilians and avoid further deterioration of the situation".

  [^cite_note-156]

  [^cite_note-157]

  &#x20;After a diplomat from the Israeli embassy criticized Beijing for not condemning Hamas, ministry spokeswoman [Mao Ning](71679066.md) renewed calls for a two-state solution and said as "a friend to both Israel and Palestine, what we hope to see is the two countries living together in peace."

  [^cite_note-158]

  [^cite_note-159]

  &#x20;On 18 October, China's UN ambassador [Zhang Jun](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zhang_Jun_\(ambassador\)) condemned the "heinous attack" on [Al-Ahli Hospital](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al-Ahli_Arab_Hospital_explosion) and urged Israel to "fulfil its obligations under international humanitarian law", adding that Palestinians had been forced to move south because of Israel's evacuation order "only to suffer from air strikes in the south as well".

  [^cite_note-160]

  &#x20;China voted against condemning Hamas at the [United Nations](31769.md).

  [^cite_note-auto4-39]

  &#x20;On 30 October, China's embassy in France posted a photo on [X](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/X_\(website\)) comparing intact buildings in [Xinjiang](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Xinjiang) with buildings in Gaza destroyed in the bombing; the photo was criticised by heads of the World Uyghur Congress and [East Turkistan Government in Exile](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/East_Turkistan_Government_in_Exile).

  [^cite_note-\:02-161]

  &#x20;\
  &#x20;Regarding an ICJ order calling on Israel to take measures to prevent genocide in Gaza, Foreign Ministry spokesman [Wang Wenbin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wang_Wenbin) on 29 January 2024 said it was a response to the international community's concern about easing the [humanitarian crisis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gaza_humanitarian_crisis_\(2023%E2%80%93present\)) there, adding: "We hope the ICJ's provisional measures can be effectively implemented."

  [^cite_note-\:16-162]

  [^cite_note-163]

  &#x20;At a news conference in Beijing on 7 March 2024, Foreign Minister [Wang Yi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wang_Yi_\(politician\)) called the conflict "a tragedy for humanity and a disgrace for civilisation",

  [^cite_note-164]

  &#x20;adding that "injustice done to the Palestinian people must be corrected."

  [^cite_note-\:16-162]

  &#x20;In May 2025, Chinese Ambassador to Israel Xiao Junzheng stated the October 7 attacks was "inhumane, unforgivable and also outrageous", adding "China opposes and condemns what Hamas did on the seventh of October", while maintaining support for a two-state solution.

  [^cite_note-165]

  &#x20;In July 2025, Xiao said that "China has made it clear and publicly that we oppose Iran’s development and possession of nuclear weapons" and that "We respect Israel’s right to subsistence and development. We are against some slogans by some people and some countries about wiping Israel off the map of the world." He added that China condemns "intrusion" by Hamas into Israeli territory, stating "international law grants occupied populations rights to employ armed resistance ending foreign occupation" but that "this stops there" and does not allow invading Israeli territory and violence against civilians.

  [^cite_note-166]

  &#x20;In August 2025, China officially referred to [genocide](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gaza_genocide_recognition) in Gaza with the U.S. as an "accomplice" and called for the "imperative of an arm embargo on Israel."

  [^cite_note-167]

  [^cite_note-\:72-168]

-  [India](14533.md): Prime Minister [Narendra Modi](444222.md) said the government condemned the "terrorist attacks on Israel", adding that "we stand in solidarity with Israel at this difficult hour".

  [^cite_note-169]

  [^cite_note-170]

  &#x20;The [Ministry Of External Affairs](10302410.md) added that while they condemned the actions of Hamas, they supported [the establishment of Palestine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Two-state_solution).

  [^cite_note-171]

  &#x20;India voted to condemn Hamas at the [United Nations](31769.md).

  [^cite_note-auto4-39]

-  [Indonesia](14579.md): The [Foreign Ministry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_Foreign_Affairs_\(Indonesia\)) issued a statement expressing its deep concern "with the escalation of conflict between Palestine and Israel", and urged the immediate end of violence to avoid further human casualties. It also called for the occupation of Palestinian territories by Israel as the root of the conflict, to be resolved in accordance with the parameters agreed upon by the United Nations.

  [^cite_note-172]

  &#x20;President [Joko Widodo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Joko_Widodo), in his address on 10 October, urged both sides to stop the conflict, deescalate the tensions, and ordered the foreign ministry to protect Indonesian nationals currently in Palestine and Israel.

  [^cite_note-173]

-  [Iran](14653.md): The Supreme Leader of Iran Ayatollah [Ali Khamenei](385653.md) said that "the U.S. is responsible for the recent crimes." He added, "If the crimes of the Zionist regime continue, no one can stop the Muslims and the resistance forces".

  [^cite_note-174]

  &#x20;Yahya Rahim Safavi, former commander of the [Islamic Revolutionary Guard Corps](1285472.md) and an adviser to the Khamenei, congratulated the Palestinian fighters, and stated that Iran would "stand by the Palestinian fighters", until the "liberation of Palestine and Jerusalem". Ayatollah [Ahmad Alamolhoda](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ahmad_Alamolhoda), a representative of Khamenei asked people to be ready to mobilize for a war between Islam and infidels.

  [^cite_note-175]

  &#x20;During a session of the [Islamic Consultative Assembly](383276.md), legislators rose from their seats and chanted "Down with Israel", "[Down with America](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Death_to_America)", and "Welcome Palestine".

  [^cite_note-176]

  &#x20;After speaking by phone with Hamas leader [Ismail Haniyeh](41207463.md) and Islamic Jihad leader [Ziyad al-Nakhalah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ziyad_al-Nakhalah), President [Ebrahim Raisi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ebrahim_Raisi) praised the "legitimate defense" of the Palestinian nation and said Israel and its supporters "must be held accountable".

  [^cite_note-177]

  &#x20;Foreign Ministry spokesperson [Nasser Kanaani](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nasser_Kanaani) said that Hamas' attack showed increased confidence by the Palestinians against Israel.

  [^cite_note-f24live-178]

  &#x20;He also denied claims of Iranian involvement in Hamas' attack.

  [^cite_note-179]

  &#x20;During a meeting with UN diplomat [Tor Wennesland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tor_Wennesland) on 14 October, foreign minister [Hossein Amir-Abdollahian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hossein_Amir-Abdollahian) warned that Iran could intervene in the war if the IDF launched a [ground invasion of Gaza](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israeli_invasion_of_the_Gaza_Strip_\(2023%E2%80%93present\)).

  [^cite_note-180]

  [^cite_note-181]

-  [Iraq](7515928.md): The official government spokesperson said that military operations undertaken by the Palestinians were a natural result of decades of "systemic oppression" by the "Zionist Occupation authority".

  [^cite_note-f24live-178]

-  [Japan](15573.md): A [Foreign Ministry](868429.md) spokesperson said that the country "strongly condemns the rocket attacks and armed incursions against Israel" and expressed "its condolences to the bereaved families and its heartfelt sympathy to the injured".

  [^cite_note-182]

-  [Jordan](7515964.md): [King Abdullah II](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/King_Abdullah_II) warned that continued escalation of the conflict would create negative repercussions on the region and urged restraint, the protection of civilians and respect for international humanitarian law.

  [^cite_note-183]

  &#x20;Foreign Minister [Ayman Safadi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ayman_Safadi) warned of the "volatility" of the situation.

  [^cite_note-\:3-184]

  &#x20;On 1 November, Jordan recalled its ambassador to Israel, accusing the country of creating an "unprecedented humanitarian catastrophe" and "killing innocent people in Gaza".

  [^cite_note-jorrecall-26]

  &#x20;It also said that Israel's ambassador, who left Amman shortly after Hamas' attack, would not be allowed to return until the war ended. Prime Minister [Bisher Khasawneh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bisher_Khasawneh) said all options were on the table in "dealing with the Israeli aggression on Gaza and its repercussions."

  [^cite_note-185]

-  [Kazakhstan](16642.md): Aibek Smadiyarov, the press secretary of the [Foreign Ministry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_Foreign_Affairs_\(Kazakhstan\)), urged all parties not to resort to actions that could escalate the situation between the two states and the Middle East region.

  [^cite_note-186]

-  [Kuwait](7515890.md): Kuwait blamed Israel for what it called its "blatant attacks".

  [^cite_note-\:3-184]

-  [Kyrgyzstan](170131.md): The Foreign Ministry called for dialogue and the cessation of hostilities.

  [^cite_note-187]

-  [Laos](17752.md): The government called for de-escalation and urged for a two-state solution.

  [^cite_note-188]

-  [Lebanon](17771.md): Lebanon voted against condemning Hamas at the [United Nations](31769.md).

  [^cite_note-auto4-39]

-  [Malaysia](3607937.md): The [Foreign Ministry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_Foreign_Affairs_\(Malaysia\)) issued a statement expressing its "concern over the latest escalation in the Middle East", blaming the root cause on "[prolonged illegal occupation](2781576.md), [blockade](19224310.md) and sufferings, the [desecration of Al-Aqsa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_Al-Aqsa_clashes), as well as the politics of dispossession at the hands of Israel as the occupier". It called for a return to the [pre-1967 border](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Green_Line_\(Israel\)) and on the [United Nations Security Council](31956.md) to "demand all parties stop the violence as well as respect and protect the lives of innocent civilians".

  [^cite_note-189]

  &#x20;Prime Minister [Anwar Ibrahim](19264815.md) also explicitly refused to condemn Hamas allegedly under pressure from Western nations, emphasizing his country's long standing relationship with the group.

  [^cite_note-190]

  &#x20;Malaysia voted against condemning Hamas at the [United Nations](31769.md).

  [^cite_note-auto4-39]

  &#x20;On 20 December, Malaysia announced a ban on Israeli-owned and Israeli-flagged ships, along with those headed for Israel, from docking at Malaysian ports.

  [^cite_note-191]

-  [Maldives](19117.md): The Foreign Ministry released a statement saying that the government considered "these deliberate acts of aggression against the innocent civilian population of Palestine a blatant disregard and violation of international humanitarian law, tantamount to a war crime," the statement said.

  [^cite_note-192]

-  [Mongolia](19271.md): The Foreign Ministry released a statement saying that the country condemned "the rocket and terror attacks from Gaza on Israel, which have resulted in deaths and injuries of many innocent civilians." It also called "on all parties concerned to immediately end violence and to protect the safety and security of civilians at all cost".

  [^cite_note-193]

-  [Myanmar](19457.md): Following the [Al-Ahli Arab Hospital explosion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al-Ahli_Arab_Hospital_explosion), the military junta expressed concern about the conflict and urged relevant parties to follow restraint.

  [^cite_note-194]

  &#x20;The opposition government of the [National League for Democracy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_League_for_Democracy), which is present at the United Nations, voted to condemn Hamas.

  [^cite_note-auto4-39]

-  [Nepal](171166.md): Prime Minister [Pushpa Kamal Dahal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pushpa_Kamal_Dahal) condemned Hamas' offensive on Israel calling it a "terrorist attack".

  [^cite_note-195]

-  [North Korea](21255.md): *[Rodong Sinmun](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rodong_Sinmun)*, the official newspaper of the ruling [Workers' Party of Korea](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Workers'_Party_of_Korea), published an editorial blaming Israel for the conflict, stating that the "international community claims that this clash was the result of Israel's constant criminal acts against the Palestinian people, and that the fundamental way out is to build an independent Palestinian state".

  [^cite_note-196]

-  [Oman](22316.md): The Foreign Ministry called on the international community to "intervene immediately to stop the ongoing escalation and resort to the rules of international law". It affirmed its support for a [Two-state solution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Two-state_solution) involving a Palestinian state with [East Jerusalem](343508.md) as its capital.

  [^cite_note-197]

-  [Pakistan](23235.md): The [Foreign Office](13048437.md) issued a statement urging an immediate ceasefire and a return to peaceful negotiations, stating that they were "closely monitoring the unfolding situation".

  [^cite_note-198]

  &#x20;Pakistan reiterated its position that the solution to the conflict was a [two-state solution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Two-state_solution), including the establishment of a viable, sovereign and contiguous State of Palestine with [Jerusalem](16043.md) as its capital.

  [^cite_note-199]

-  [Philippines](23440.md): The [Office of President](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Office_of_the_President_of_the_Philippines) [Bongbong Marcos](572460.md) issued a statement conveying "its deepest sympathies and condolences to those who have lost their family members and loved ones in recent attacks". It also condemned the attack and said that "the Philippines understands the right of states to self-defense in the light of external aggression as recognized in the [United Nations Charter](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Nations_Charter)."

  [^cite_note-200]

  &#x20;The [National Security Council](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Security_Council_\(Philippines\)) proposed to [officially designate](63578.md) Hamas as a terrorist organization.

  [^cite_note-201]

  &#x20;The Philippines abstained from voting for a United Nations General Assembly resolution calling for a humanitarian truce in Gaza, due to the lack of explicit condemnation on Hamas' initial attacks on 7 October.

  [^cite_note-202]

  -  [Bangsamoro](37260549.md): The [Bangsamoro Parliament](42303279.md) on 17 October passed a resolution condemning acts of violence and "collective punishment" against Palestinians and urged for unconditional cessation of hostilities between Israel and Hamas and for both sides to allow the United Nations to establish a humanitarian corridor. It also said that the conflict should not be viewed in isolation and should be understood in the wider context of the decades-long Palestinian struggle towards self-determination.

    [^cite_note-203]

    &#x20;On 16 November, the parliament passed a resolution to reiterate its call for "ceasefire now".

    [^cite_note-204]

-  [Qatar](8238258.md): The [Foreign Ministry](35709864.md) issued a statement saying Israel was "solely responsible for the ongoing escalation due to its continuous violations of the rights of the Palestinian people, including the recent repeated incursions into the Al-Aqsa Mosque under the protection of the Israeli police."

  [^cite_note-205]

-  [Saudi Arabia](349303.md): The [Foreign Ministry](31639195.md) released a statement calling for an "immediate halt" to the "escalation" in Israel and Gaza. It added they were "closely following developments in the unprecedented situation between a number of Palestinian factions and the Israel Defense Forces, which has resulted in a high level of violence taking place on a number of fronts there" and reiterated "its repeated warnings \[to Israel] of the dangers of the explosion of the situation as a result of the continued occupation, and deprivation of the Palestinian people of their legitimate rights, and the repetition of systematic provocations against its sanctities".

  [^cite_note-206]

  [^cite_note-207]

-  [Singapore](27318.md): A spokesperson for the [Foreign Ministry](2552683.md) issued a statement saying that the country strongly condemned "the rocket and terror attacks from Gaza on Israel."

  [^cite_note-208]

  &#x20;On 18 October, Prime Minister [Lee Hsien Loong](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lee_Hsien_Loong), President [Tharman Shanmugaratnam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tharman_Shanmugaratnam) and Foreign Affairs Minister [Vivian Balakrishnan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vivian_Balakrishnan) sent condolence letters to their Palestinian counterparts after the [Al-Ahli Hospital explosion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al-Ahli_Arab_Hospital_explosion) and reiterated support the two-state solution.

  [^cite_note-209]

  &#x20;In response to a 11 November op-ed in *[The Straits Times](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Straits_Times)* by retired diplomat [Bilahari Kausikan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bilahari_Kausikan), [Home Affairs Minister](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_Home_Affairs_\(Singapore\)) [K. Shanmugam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/K._Shanmugam) noted that Kausikan did not emphasise the illegal actions by Israel, saying: "The truth is that Israeli government actions have been contrary to international law, and oppressive to the Palestinians. One can say this while also accepting that parts of the Palestinian leadership has acted very badly, seeking the complete wipe-out of Israel."

  [^cite_note-210]

-  [South Korea](27019.md): The Foreign Ministry issued a statement saying that the government strongly condemned "the indiscriminate attacks on Israel from Gaza including rocket attacks", and called for "an immediate halt" to them.

  [^cite_note-211]

-  [Sri Lanka](26750.md): The [Foreign Ministry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_Foreign_Affairs_\(Sri_Lanka\)) expressed concern about the loss of life in both Israel and Gaza.

  [^cite_note-212]

  [^cite_note-213]

  &#x20;President [Ranil Wickremesinghe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ranil_Wickremesinghe) denounced Hamas's aggression, stating that [criticism of Israel](30074296.md) is not a valid justification for Hamas's attack.

  [^cite_note-214]

  &#x20;Sri Lanka voted against condemning Hamas at the [United Nations](31769.md).

  [^cite_note-auto4-39]

-  [Syria](57134800.md): The Foreign Ministry said an agreement to normalize relations between Saudi Arabia and Israel was now impossible.

  [^cite_note-215]

  &#x20;Syria voted against condemning Hamas at the [United Nations](31769.md).

  [^cite_note-auto4-39]

-  [Taiwan](25734.md): The [Foreign Ministry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_Foreign_Affairs_\(Taiwan\)) issued a statement saying that the country strongly condemned "the indiscriminate attacks against Israelis carried out by Hamas." It also said Taiwan stood "in solidarity with Israel" and denounced "all forms of terrorism", adding that their thoughts were "with the victims & families at this tragic juncture."

  [^cite_note-216]

  [^cite_note-217]

  &#x20;On 23 October 2023, Taiwanese representative to Israel [Lee Ya-Ping](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lee_Ya-Ping?action=edit\&redlink=1) donated US\$70,000 to Israeli NGO Pitchon-Lev as aid to Israeli soldiers and families affected by the war.

  [^cite_note-218]

  [^cite_note-219]

  &#x20;Taiwanese President [Tsai Ing-wen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tsai_Ing-wen) and Foreign Minister [Joseph Wu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Joseph_Wu) condemned the [April 2024 Iranian strikes against Israel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/April_2024_Iranian_strikes_against_Israel).

  [^cite_note-220]

  [^cite_note-221]

-  [Tajikistan](30108.md): The Foreign Ministry strongly condemned the acts of violence and called on the parties to immediately end the violence and begin dialogue to normalize the situation to prevent an escalation of tensions in the Middle East.

  [^cite_note-222]

  &#x20;Tajikistan voted against condemning Hamas at the [United Nations](31769.md).

  [^cite_note-auto4-39]

-  [Thailand](30128.md): Prime Minister [Srettha Thavisin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Srettha_Thavisin) expressed his deepest condolences to the government and people of Israel, and condemned Hamas' attack.

  [^cite_note-223]

  &#x20;He also put the [Royal Thai Air Force](1778758.md) on standby to evacuate its citizens if needed.

  [^cite_note-auto-224]

  &#x20;Deputy foreign affairs minister Jakkapong Sangmanee later said that the country's position was "one of neutrality" and that the government favoured "a solution that would allow Palestine and Israel to coexist."

  [^cite_note-225]

-  [Timor-Leste](19159399.md): Timor-Leste voted to condemn Hamas at the [United Nations](31769.md).

  [^cite_note-auto4-39]

-  [United Arab Emirates](69328.md): The Foreign Ministry called for an immediate ceasefire.

  [^cite_note-226]

  &#x20;Later it condemned Hamas for its "serious and grave escalation" and its hostage taking.

  [^cite_note-227]

-  [Uzbekistan](31853.md): The Foreign Ministry called on the warring parties to take all measures to quickly end the armed confrontation and resolve the crisis through political and diplomatic means.

  [^cite_note-228]

  [^cite_note-229]

-  [Vietnam](202354.md): [Foreign Ministry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_Foreign_Affairs_\(Vietnam\)) spokesperson Phạm Thu Hằng said that the country was "deeply concerned over the escalating violence between Hamas and Israel \[that has led to massive civilian casualties]" and called on relevant parties "exercise restraint, refrain from taking actions that complicate the situation, promtply resume negotiations to settle disagreements through peaceful measures, on the basis of [international law](8195726.md) and [related resolutions of the United Nations Security Council](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_United_Nations_resolutions_concerning_Palestine), and ensure safety and legitimate interests of civilians."

  [^cite_note-230]

  &#x20;In a followed statement, the same figure stated that Vietnam "strongly condemns [acts of violence against civilians](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Determinants_of_violence_against_civilians), humanitarian facilities, and essential infrastructure in the Middle East conflict", while calling on both parties to "immediately [cease fire](592591.md), end the use of force, respect [international humanitarian law](1093604.md), [resume negotiations, and resolve differences through peaceful means](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israeli%E2%80%93Palestinian_peace_process)."

  [^cite_note-231]

-  [Yemen](350939.md): The [Foreign Ministry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_Foreign_Affairs_\(Yemen\)) confirmed that the government was following the course of events and escalation taking place in the occupied Palestinian territories, and called for the protection of civilians and an end to the provocations of the Israel Defense Forces and their repeated attacks on the Palestinian people and their sanctities. It also affirmed the government's "firm" position to help achieve the aspirations of the Palestinian people for a decent life, and the establishment of their independent Palestinian state with [East Jerusalem](343508.md) as its capital, in accordance with the [Arab Peace Initiative](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arab_Peace_Initiative) and relevant international resolutions and legislation.

  [^cite_note-\:1-232]

  [^cite_note-\:2-55]

### Europe

See also: [European Union reactions to the Gaza war](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/European_Union_reactions_to_the_Gaza_war), [German support for Israel in the Gaza war](78816942.md), and [United Kingdom and the Gaza war](76740255.md)

-  [Albania](738.md): Prime Minister [Edi Rama](277805.md) and Foreign Minister [Igli Hasani](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Igli_Hasani) expressed strong condemnation of the attack and expressed solidarity with the victims, their families and all of Israel.

  [^cite_note-233]

  [^cite_note-234]

  [^cite_note-235]

-  [Andorra](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Andorra): Prime Minister [Xavier Espot](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Xavier_Espot) issued a statement condemning the "terrorist attacks perpetrated in various locations in Israel", expressing "solidarity with the Israeli authorities and civilian population", as well as a call to "stop the escalation of violence and respect international humanitarian law and deploy all diplomatic efforts to protect the civilian populations of both Israel and Palestine".

  [^cite_note-236]

-  [Armenia](10918072.md): The Foreign Ministry expressed its shock at the violence between Palestinians and Israelis and the targeting of civilians, saying that it was joining "the international community demanding an end to the violence".

  [^cite_note-237]

-  [Austria](26964606.md): Foreign Minister [Alexander Schallenberg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alexander_Schallenberg) announced the suspension of the delivery of 19 million euros (\$20 million) of aid to Palestinian areas on 9 October in response to Hamas' attack and said that it would review its existing projects in Palestine. He also said that he would summon the Iranian ambassador to address Iran's "abhorrent reactions" to the attack.

  [^cite_note-238]



Azerbaijani President [Ilham Aliyev](22765442.md) with Israeli President Isaac Herzog, 16 February 2024

-  [Azerbaijan](746.md): The Foreign Ministry condemned "violence against civilians in the Israel-Palestine conflict zone" and called "for an urgent de-escalation of the situation".

  [^cite_note-239]

  [^cite_note-240]

  &#x20;On 9 October, Azerbaijani Ambassador to Israel Mukhtar Mammadov condemned the "rocket strikes and terrorist attacks", adding he was appalled by numerous abductions of civilians, including women and children, who were abused by terrorist abductors.

  [^cite_note-241]

  [^cite_note-242]

  &#x20;In February 2024, Azerbaijani President [Ilham Aliyev](22765442.md) met with Israeli President Isaac Herzog, reaffirming bilateral relations between Israel and Azerbaijan amid the ongoing war in Gaza.

  [^cite_note-243]

  &#x20;Azerbaijan is a [major oil supplier](17808102.md) to Israel and has resisted pressure to cut ties with Israel over the Gaza war.

  [^cite_note-244]

-  [Belarus](3457.md): The government called for restraint and dialogue by both parties.

  [^cite_note-245]

  &#x20;President [Alexander Lukashenko](156589.md) criticized claims that Russia and Belarus had benefited from the situation through attention shifting away from the [Russo-Ukrainian War](42085878.md), instead suggesting that the United States was the beneficiary of the conflict, and stated that Israeli Prime Minister [Benjamin Netanyahu](56472.md) had been the "target of attacks for months and years" in domestic Israeli politics. He warned against further escalation, describing it as a "dangerous situation".

  [^cite_note-246]

-  [Belgium](3343.md): Foreign Minister [Hadja Lahbib](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hadja_Lahbib) said on X that the country strongly condemned "the massive rocket attacks against Israeli civilians" and said that their "thoughts are with all those affected".

  [^cite_note-\:4-112]

  &#x20;On 8 November, Deputy Prime Minister [Petra De Sutter](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Petra_De_Sutter) called on the Belgian government to adopt sanctions against Israel and investigate the bombings of hospitals and refugee camps in Gaza.

  [^cite_note-247]

  &#x20;Likewise, Prime Minister [Alexander De Croo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alexander_De_Croo) called the Israeli bombing campaign in Gaza "disproportionate", but said that "Belgium will not take sides".

  [^cite_note-248]

  &#x20;On 23 November 2023, Prime Minister De Croo along with Spanish Prime Minister Pedro Sánchez held a [press conference](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Press_conference) at the [Rafah border crossing](10475618.md), emphasizing the importance of Israel's adherence to [international humanitarian law](1093604.md).

  [^cite_note-\:14-249]

-  [Bosnia and Herzegovina](3463.md): [Chairwoman of the Council of Ministers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chairman_of_the_Council_of_Ministers_of_Bosnia_and_Herzegovina) [Borjana Krišto](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Borjana_Kri%C5%A1to) condemned Hamas' attacks as "unjust and brutal" and expressed support for Israel.

  [^cite_note-250]

  &#x20;Minister of Foreign Affairs [Elmedin Konaković](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Elmedin_Konakovi%C4%87) condemned Hamas as well, but also said that he never hid his "support for the people and government of Palestine in order to keep and protect the areas they live in, to fight for sovereignty and territorial integrity of their land and protect their religious objects on that area that are of great importance for the faithful from all around the world."

  [^cite_note-251]

  - [Federation of Bosnia and Herzegovina](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Federation_of_Bosnia_and_Herzegovina):&#x20;

    -  [Canton 10](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Canton_10): The cantonal government expressed shock and sadness at "the terrorist attack on the State of Israel", at the same time, the cantonal government issued a statement of support for Israel and "its efforts to establish peace for all its residents by all legitimate means".

      [^cite_note-252]

    -  [Sarajevo Canton](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sarajevo_Canton): Cantonal Prime Minister [Nihad Uk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nihad_Uk) "expressed sorrow and compassion" for Palestinians and offered the cantonal government's help "for the people in the occupied Gaza".

      [^cite_note-253]

      - [Sarajevo](26786.md): Mayor [Benjamina Karić](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Benjamina_Kari%C4%87) said it was "hypocritical to condemn Hamas' attack on Israel, and not to condemn all that happened before and after it".

        [^cite_note-254]

  -  [Republika Srpska](347422.md): President [Milorad Dodik](4218129.md) condemned the attacks and expressed his support to Israel. The Palace of President of the Republic was decorated with the Israeli flag on 8 October.

    [^cite_note-255]

-  [Bulgaria](3415.md): Prime Minister [Nikolai Denkov](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nikolai_Denkov) expressed his country's solidarity with the Israeli people and condemned Hamas's attack.

  [^cite_note-256]

  &#x20;The [National Assembly](2122384.md) building was lit up in the colors of the Israeli flag.

  [^cite_note-landmark-257]

-  [Croatia](5573.md): Prime Minister [Andrej Plenković](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Andrej_Plenkovi%C4%87) condemned the attacks referring to them as "terrorist acts against civilians" and expressed solidarity with Israel.

  [^cite_note-258]

  &#x20;Foreign Minister [Gordan Grlić-Radman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gordan_Grli%C4%87-Radman) also condemned the attack and expressed solidarity with Israel.

  [^cite_note-259]

  &#x20;Following Israeli attacks on Gaza, president [Zoran Milanović](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zoran_Milanovi%C4%87) publicly stated that Israel had lost his sympathy due to its humanitarian crimes and "reprisal actions".

  [^cite_note-260]

  &#x20;He said on 18 October that the Palestinians deserve to have a state, which should have been respected a long time ago, and most of these horrors and ordeals would not have happened.

  [^cite_note-261]

-  [Cyprus](5593.md): President [Nikos Christodoulides](58652814.md) and the Cypriot embassy in Israel condemned the attacks and stated that Cyprus stood with Israel.

  [^cite_note-262]

  [^cite_note-263]

-  [Czech Republic](5321.md): Prime Minister [Petr Fiala](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Petr_Fiala) condemned the attack, stating his thoughts were "with the innocent victims of the violence" and wishing "our friends in Israel the swiftest possible handling of the situation and the fulfilment of their ambitions to live in peace and security."

  [^cite_note-264]

  [^cite_note-265]

  &#x20;Fiala participated in pro-Israel demonstrations in Prague and called the country "the voice of Israel in Europe."

  [^cite_note-266]

  &#x20;Czech Defense Minister [Jana Černochová](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jana_%C4%8Cernochov%C3%A1), in response to the [ceasefire resolution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Nations_General_Assembly_Resolution_ES-10/21), called for the Czech Republic to withdraw from the UN,

  [^cite_note-267]

  &#x20;which was rejected by Prime Minister Fiala, Foreign Minister [Jan Lipavský](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jan_Lipavsk%C3%BD) and other cabinet officials.

  [^cite_note-268]

-  [Denmark](76972.md): [Foreign Minister](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Minister_of_Foreign_Affairs_\(Denmark\)) [Lars Løkke Rasmussen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lars_L%C3%B8kke_Rasmussen) strongly condemned the attack against Israel, adding that his thoughts were "with the victims, their families and all of Israel".

  [^cite_note-269]

-  [Estonia](28222445.md): [Marko Mihkelson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marko_Mihkelson), the Chairman of the Foreign Affairs Committee of the [Riigikogu](794927.md), strongly condemned the attacks against Israel, saying he was "concerned about the expansion of terrorist activity in the region". He invited international organisations to "confront the attempts of terrorist regimes to create instability and chaos in the whole Middle East region."

  [^cite_note-270]

-  [Finland](10577.md): [Foreign Minister](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Minister_of_Foreign_Affairs_\(Finland\)) [Elina Valtonen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Elina_Valtonen) condemned in the strongest terms the "terrorist rocket attacks targeting Israel".

  [^cite_note-271]



French President [Emmanuel Macron](43671127.md) with Israeli President [Isaac Herzog](4645588.md) in Jerusalem, 24 October 2023

-  [France](5843419.md): President [Emmanuel Macron](43671127.md) strongly condemned the attack and expressed his "full solidarity with the victims, their families and loved ones".

  [^cite_note-272]

  [^cite_note-\:4-112]

  &#x20;The French embassy in Israel condemned the attacks and described Hamas' actions as "inadmissible terrorist attacks".

  [^cite_note-273]

  &#x20;On 25 October 2023, Macron said a "massive intervention that would put civilian lives at risk would be an error".

  [^cite_note-france24-invasion-45]

  &#x20;On 10 November 2023, Macron called for a ceasefire and urged Israel to stop [bombing Gaza and killing civilians](75113830.md).

  [^cite_note-274]

  &#x20;On 17 December 2023, French foreign minister [Catherine Colonna](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Catherine_Colonna) called for an immediate ceasefire, adding that too many civilians were being killed in Gaza.

  [^cite_note-275]

  [^cite_note-276]

  &#x20;On 20 December, Macron stated fighting terrorism did not mean flattening Gaza or attacking "civilian populations indiscriminately".

  [^cite_note-277]

  &#x20;In January 2024, Macron accused Hamas of using Palestinian civilians as [human shields](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Human_shield) and said Israel had the right to defend itself.

  [^cite_note-278]

  &#x20;In July 2025, France together with more than two dozen Western countries including the United Kingdom called for an immediate end to the war in Gaza in a joint statement.

  [^cite_note-\:28-279]

  &#x20;In September 2026, French Foreign Minister [Jean‑Noël Barrot](54388162.md) announced on X that France, together with 11 other nations, would cease all commercial trade with Israeli settlements in the occupied Palestinian territories. Explaining the rationale behind the move, Barrot pointed to the "frenzied expansion of the settlements," a surge in violence carried out by extremist settlers against Palestinians, and acts of terrorism that, he noted, had also been condemned by Israeli authorities themselves. He further warned that the situation in the West Bank was "close to exploding," and stressed that the international community could no longer afford to stand by.

  [^cite_note-280]

-  [Georgia](48768.md): The [Foreign Ministry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_Foreign_Affairs_of_Georgia) strongly condemned the attack on Israel, and expressed solidarity with the Israeli government and people. The ministry also offered thoughts and condolences and wished a speedy recovery to the injured.

  [^cite_note-281]

  

  German Chancellor [Olaf Scholz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Olaf_Scholz) with Israeli President [Isaac Herzog](4645588.md) in Tel Aviv, Israel, 17 October 2023



German President [Frank-Walter Steinmeier](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Frank-Walter_Steinmeier) with Israeli President Herzog in Jerusalem, 26 November 2023

-  [Germany](11867.md): Chancellor [Olaf Scholz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Olaf_Scholz) said he was deeply shocked by the "terrifying news" of "rocket fire from Gaza and the escalating violence". He also said that Germany condemned Hamas' attack and would stand by Israel."

  [^cite_note-282]

  [^cite_note-\:4-112]

  &#x20;Germany suspended €125 million (\$131 million) of development aid to Palestine in response to Hamas' attack and said it would review other projects and aid given.

  [^cite_note-283]

  &#x20;Scholz offered military aid to Israel.

  [^cite_note-euronews-arms-284]

  &#x20;Scholz later announced his opposition to an "immediate ceasefire" in Gaza, saying "that would mean ultimately that Israel leaves Hamas the possibility of recovering and obtaining new missiles," and called instead for "humanitarian pauses".

  [^cite_note-285]

  &#x20;On 17 December 2023, [Minister for Foreign Affairs](1329748.md) [Annalena Baerbock](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Annalena_Baerbock) said she would "support a ceasefire, but only if it is sustainable".

  [^cite_note-286]

  &#x20;President [Frank-Walter Steinmeier](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Frank-Walter_Steinmeier) called on [Arabs living in Germany](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arabs_in_Germany) to distance themselves from Hamas.

  [^cite_note-287]

-  [Greece](12108.md): Prime Minister [Kyriakos Mitsotakis](2780816.md) strongly condemned the terrorist attack against Israel, that caused a high number of casualties among civilians. He also expressed his full support for the people of Israel and its right to self-defense.

  [^cite_note-288]

  &#x20;Migration minister [Dimitris Keridis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dimitris_Keridis?action=edit\&redlink=1) expressed his concerns of a resurgence in illegal immigrants to southern Europe, saying that the attack would "not ease illegal migration".

  [^cite_note-289]

  &#x20;On 18 January 2024, Prime Minister Mitsotakis asked Israel "not to create a new generation of orphans".

  [^cite_note-290]

-  [Hungary](13275.md): Prime Minister [Viktor Orbán](750582.md) strongly condemned the attack against Israel, and stated that he unequivocally supported Israel's right to self-defence. He also expressed his "sympathy and condolences" to Prime Minister [Benjamin Netanyahu](56472.md), adding that "our thoughts and prayers are with the people of Israel in these dark hours".

  [^cite_note-291]

  [^cite_note-292]

-  [Iceland](14531.md): Foreign Minister [Þórdís Kolbrún R. Gylfadóttir](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/%C3%9E%C3%B3rd%C3%ADs_Kolbr%C3%BAn_R._Gylfad%C3%B3ttir) condemned the attack and said that Israel has the right to defend itself.

  [^cite_note-293]

-  [Ireland](14560.md): Taoiseach [Leo Varadkar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Leo_Varadkar) strongly condemned Hamas' attacks against Israel, calling them "appalling" and expressed that Ireland unequivocally condemned attacks on civilians. [Tánaiste](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/T%C3%A1naiste), Foreign Minister, and Defence Minister [Micheál Martin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Miche%C3%A1l_Martin) further condemned Hamas' "unconscionable" attacks, expressing that "deliberate and systematic targeting of civilians can never be justified". Both Varadkar and Martin called for an immediate cessation of all hostilities.

  [^cite_note-294]

  &#x20;President [Michael D. Higgins](559753.md) subsequently issued a statement condemning the attacks as "deeply reprehensible" and expressed his support for Israel's right to defend itself.

  [^cite_note-295]

  [^cite_note-296]
