A new show business generation is beginning to challenge that dominant ideology. And of course the old guard will do everything in their power to intimidate them."

  [^cite_note-guardian-2023-12-02-540]

- [Marcia Cross](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marcia_Cross) wrote an Instagram post with broken heart and Palestinian flag emojis, stating, "I'm struggling to comprehend how to live among people with eyes that don't water, hearts that don't flinch, and voices that remain silent. There are no words for the horror that has and is being unleashed. And the silence has me believing I am deaf."

  [^cite_note-541]

- [Selma Blair](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Selma_Blair) called pro-Palestinian activists "terrorist supporting goons" and advocated for their [deportation](412990.md), stating, "May they meet their fate."

  [^cite_note-542]

- [Joe Rogan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Joe_Rogan) described Israel's actions in the [Gaza Strip](12047.md) as a "[genocide](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Allegations_of_genocide_in_the_2023_Israeli_attack_on_Gaza)" in a discussion with comedian [Kurt Metzger](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kurt_Metzger).

  [^cite_note-543]

  &#x20;In a podcast with [Coleman Hughes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coleman_Hughes) eight days later during which Hughes disputed the genocide allegations, Rogan said: "I see what you're saying. You clearly know more about it than I do."

  [^cite_note-544]

  [^cite_note-545]

- [Chris Martin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chris_Martin) of British rock band [Coldplay](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coldplay) stated during a concert in November 2023 that "We don't believe in oppression, or occupation, terrorism or genocide", asking the public to send love to those going through difficulties.

  [^cite_note-546]

  &#x20;In June 2024, Coldplay invited Palestinian-Chilean singer [Elyanna](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Elyanna) to perform the songs "[We Pray](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/We_Pray)" and "[Arabesque](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arabesque_\(Coldplay_song\))" with them at the [Glastonbury Festival](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Glastonbury_Festival_2024).

  [^cite_note-547]

- [Floyd Mayweather Jr.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Floyd_Mayweather_Jr.) expressed support and sent military equipment to Israel.

  [^cite_note-548]

- British rapper [Lowkey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lowkey) used his [Instagram](31591547.md) profile to raise awareness regarding the death of 91 children that were killed during Israel's attacks on Gaza.

  [^cite_note-549]

- [Iranian opposition](80207658.md) activist and analyst at [Kayhan London](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kayhan_London) Bahram Farrokhi believed that Israel's conduct in the war "does nothing to free the hostages or end terror, but razes entire neighborhoods to the ground and breeds new generation of radicalized youth". He also stressed the importance of defending Israel's "inalienable right to existence, in security and peace" and establishing peace based on "dialogue between peoples".

  [^cite_note-550]

### Political figures

-  [Argentina](18951905.md): During the second [presidential debate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Presidential_debates_in_Argentina) for the [2023 presidential election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_Argentine_presidential_election) which took place the day after the start of the conflict on 8 October, candidates [Javier Milei](56599840.md), [Patricia Bullrich](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Patricia_Bullrich), [Sergio Massa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sergio_Massa) and [Juan Schiaretti](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Juan_Schiaretti) all voiced support for Israel; the only exception was left-wing candidate [Myriam Bregman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Myriam_Bregman) (who is [Jewish](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/History_of_the_Jews_in_Argentina)), who made an anti-Zionist speech.

  [^cite_note-551]

  &#x20;Massa promised in the debate that, if elected president, he would put Hamas on the list of terrorist organizations in Argentina.

  [^cite_note-552]

  &#x20;Both Bullrich and [Buenos Aires](255919.md) mayor [Horacio Rodríguez Larreta](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Horacio_Rodr%C3%ADguez_Larreta) later participated in a pro-Israel rally in the capital a day later.

  [^cite_note-553]

-  [Bolivia](3462.md): Former president [Evo Morales](284163.md) supported the Palestinian militant groups and criticized the Bolivian government's response.

  [^cite_note-554]

  &#x20;Former ambassador to the UN [Sacha Llorenti](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sacha_Llorenti) demonstrated in support of Palestine on the streets of [La Paz](42821.md) and called on Israel to be declared "a terrorist state".

  [^cite_note-555]

-  [Canada](5042916.md): Leader of the Opposition [Pierre Poilievre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pierre_Poilievre) condemned the invasion of Israel as "terrorism and sadistic violence", and stated that Israel had the right to defend itself against these attacks.

  [^cite_note-CTV_Lee_7_October-87]

  &#x20;Former Prime Minister [Stephen Harper](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stephen_Harper) expressed his solidarity with Israel.

  [^cite_note-556]

-  [France](5843419.md): Leader of the left-wing [La France Insoumise](52520313.md) and former presidential candidate [Jean-Luc Mélenchon](20298042.md) criticized the [Conseil Représentatif des Institutions juives de France](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Conseil_Repr%C3%A9sentatif_des_Institutions_juives_de_France) for enforcing the agenda of Israel's government, which he described as far right, after the latter held rallies supporting Israel.

  [^cite_note-557]

  &#x20;Prior to that, his party referred to Hamas' attack as "an armed offensive of Palestinian forces".

  [^cite_note-bbcfrance-558]

  &#x20;A scheduled "grand civic march" called by the [French parliament](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/French_parliament) on 12 November against rising [antisemitism in France](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Antisemitism_in_France) following the war was marred by controversy after [Marine Le Pen](572897.md), the leader of the far-right [National Rally](67119.md) attended along with party president [Jordan Bardella](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jordan_Bardella), given her father and predecessor as party leader, [Jean-Marie Le Pen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jean-Marie_Le_Pen)'s history of antisemitic and [Holocaust denialist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Holocaust_denial) statements. Melenchon, who did not attend the march, called it a meeting of "friends of unconditional support for the massacre" in Gaza.

  [^cite_note-559]

  [^cite_note-560]

-  [Iraq](7515928.md): Iraq's top [Shia](26961.md) Muslim leader, [Ali al-Sistani](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ali_al-Sistani), condemned Israel and called on everyone in the world to stand up to the "terrible brutality" in besieged Gaza.

  [^cite_note-561]

-  [Philippines](23440.md): Former president [Rodrigo Duterte](7407293.md) said in an interview that if he was Netanyahu, he would "pulverise" Gaza and render it "the biggest cemetery in the world".

  [^cite_note-562]

-  [United Kingdom](31717.md): Keir Starmer, then [Leader of the Opposition](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Leader_of_the_Opposition_\(United_Kingdom\)), condemned the attacks and said that "Israel has a right to defend herself".

  [^cite_note-563]

  [^cite_note-564]

  &#x20;He later announced his support for Israel's "right" to totally cut power and water supplies to Gaza in an interview with [LBC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/LBC), prompting the [Labour Muslim Network](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Labour_Muslim_Network) to describe his comments as endorsing "collective punishment" and demand an apology from him.

  [^cite_note-565]

  &#x20;Starmer's predecessor [Jeremy Corbyn](415036.md) described Hamas' attack as "alarming", urging an immediate ceasefire and calling for Israel to end the occupation of Palestine as "the only means of achieving a just and lasting peace".

  [^cite_note-566]

  &#x20;[Ben Wallace](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ben_Wallace_\(politician\)), former [Secretary of State for Defence](302332.md), stated that Israel's war "tactics will fuel the conflict for another 50 years \[and] are radicalising Muslim youth across the globe."

  [^cite_note-567]

  &#x20;Former Chief of the Defense Staff [David Richards](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/David_Richards,_Baron_Richards_of_Herstmonceux) called on Foreign Secretary David Cameron to demand a ceasefire.

  [^cite_note-568]



Bipartisan US Congressional Delegation to Israel, 12 November 2023

-  [United States](3434750.md): Former presidents [Bill Clinton](3356.md) and [Barack Obama](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Barack_Obama) condemned the attacks,

  [^cite_note-569]

  &#x20;while then-former president [Donald Trump](4848272.md) accused the Iranian government of supporting Hamas' attack and suggested that the Biden administration's agreement to unfreeze \$6 billion in Iranian funds in exchange for the release of prisoners in September 2023 could have contributed to the attack.

  [^cite_note-570]

  &#x20;Obama warned Israel that its actions could "harden Palestinian attitudes for generations" and weaken international support for Israel; any military strategy that ignored the war's human costs "could ultimately backfire".

  [^cite_note-571]

  &#x20;Former President [George W. Bush](3414021.md) warned that Israel's ground offense in Gaza was "going to be ugly for a while".

  [^cite_note-572]

  &#x20;\
  &#x20;[Hillary Clinton](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hillary_Clinton) opposed a ceasefire in Gaza, stating "Remember, there was a ceasefire on October 6, that Hamas broke by their barbaric assault on peaceful civilians," then she added "Hamas have consistently broken ceasefires over a number of years."

  [^cite_note-573]

  &#x20;Senator and former presidential candidate [Bernie Sanders](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bernie_Sanders) said that "the tragedy of what Hamas did ... is that it will feed the extremism ... into the future" adding that it would "only harden the extreme right in Israel, maybe get some encouragement to terrorists in the Palestinian area, and inflame what already has been a terrible, terrible situation."

  [^cite_note-574]

  &#x20;In addition, Sanders rejected calls for a ceasefire, stating "I don't know how you can have a ceasefire with an organization like Hamas, which is dedicated to turmoil and chaos and destroying the State of Israel." Furthermore, he told CNN "I think what the Arab countries in the region understand is that Hamas has got to go."

  [^cite_note-575]

  \
  &#x20;On 7 November, [Democrat](5043544.md) [Rashida Tlaib](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rashida_Tlaib), the first [Palestinian-American](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palestinian_Americans) member of the [US Congress](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/US_Congress), was censured by the [Republican](32070.md)-controlled [US House of Representatives](19468510.md) after she allegedly made antisemitic statements supporting the Palestinian cause. Tlaib denied the allegation, saying that deeming criticism of the Israeli government as antisemitic "sets a very dangerous precedent" which was being "used to silence diverse voices speaking up for human rights".

  [^cite_note-576]

  \
  &#x20;On 14 December, Sanders introduced a privileged resolution invoking Section 502(b) of the [Foreign Assistance Act](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Foreign_Assistance_Act), calling on the State Department to investigate [Israeli crimes against humanity](75113830.md) in its conduct of the war in Gaza.

  [^cite_note-577]

  &#x20;The resolution would freeze [U.S. military aid to Israel](65284610.md) unless the State Department issues a report within 30 days.

  [^cite_note-578]

  &#x20;The proposal was defeated, 72 to 11, with only Sanders, Democratic Senators [Jeff Merkley](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeff_Merkley), [Chris Van Hollen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chris_Van_Hollen), [Martin Heinrich](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Martin_Heinrich), [Laphonza Butler](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Laphonza_Butler), [Ed Markey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ed_Markey), [Ben Ray Luján](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ben_Ray_Luj%C3%A1n), [Mazie Hirono](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mazie_Hirono), [Peter Welch](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Peter_Welch) and [Elizabeth Warren](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Elizabeth_Warren) and Republican [Rand Paul](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rand_Paul) voting for it. Among the Senators who voted against the resolution were [Chuck Schumer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chuck_Schumer) (D-NY), [Mitch McConnell](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mitch_McConnell) (R-KY), [Dick Durbin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dick_Durbin) (D-IL), [Bill Cassidy](9386382.md) (R-LA), [Chris Murphy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chris_Murphy) (D-CT), [Rick Scott](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rick_Scott) (R-FL), [Tom Carper](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tom_Carper) (D-DE), [Mike Braun](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mike_Braun) (R-IN), [Bob Casey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bob_Casey_Jr.) (D-PA), [Mike Lee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mike_Lee) (R-UT), and Independent [Angus King](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Angus_King) of Maine and [Kyrsten Sinema](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kyrsten_Sinema) of Arizona. Senator [Brian Schatz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brian_Schatz) (D-HI) voiced support for the resolution.

  [^cite_note-579]

  [^cite_note-580]

  &#x20;Senator [Chris Coons](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chris_Coons) (D-DE) opposed the resolution. Senator [Ben Cardin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ben_Cardin) (D-MD), the chairman of the Foreign Relations Committee, said that resolution "would be a gift to Hamas, a gift to Iran."

  [^cite_note-581]

  \
  &#x20;[New York City](645042.md) Mayor [Eric Adams](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eric_Adams) opposed a ceasefire in Gaza,

  [^cite_note-582]

  &#x20;stating "Bring the hostages home."

  [^cite_note-583]

-  [Uruguay](31843.md): Former president [Julio María Sanguinetti](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Julio_Mar%C3%ADa_Sanguinetti) condemned Hamas's aggression and declared that it "must be repudiated", and that Israel has "the right to self-defense".

  [^cite_note-584]

  &#x20;On the other hand, former President [José Mujica](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jos%C3%A9_Mujica) called the conflict a "disgrace" for both the Israeli and Palestinian people, and said that the scale of violence is a "failure of humanity and the policies of the United Nations."

  [^cite_note-585]

- [Kurdistan Communities Union](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kurdistan_Communities_Union) co-chair [Cemîl Bayik](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cem%C3%AEl_Bayik) said the Israeli state and government "must abandon their policies of war, genocide and massacre", adding that the Palestinian people "have never been [anti-Semitic](1078.md)" and "have been resisting occupation and genocide for decades".

  [^cite_note-586]

  [^cite_note-587]

### Open letters

Further information: [Artists4Ceasefire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Artists4Ceasefire)

[Chris Pine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chris_Pine), [Mark Hamill](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mark_Hamill), [Billy Porter](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Billy_Porter), [Ziggy Marley](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ziggy_Marley),[^cite_note-588] [Mekhi Phifer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mekhi_Phifer), [Andy Garcia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Andy_Garcia), [Tracy-Ann Oberman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tracy-Ann_Oberman), [George Lopez](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/George_Lopez), [Ryan Murphy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ryan_Murphy_\(producer\)), [Dana Goldberg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dana_Goldberg_\(comedian\)), [Jason Alexander](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jason_Alexander), [Eli Roth](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eli_Roth), [Ryan Kavanaugh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ryan_Kavanaugh), [Jeremy Piven](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeremy_Piven), [Aaron Bay-Schuck](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aaron_Bay-Schuck), [Marty Singer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marty_Singer), [Antoine Fuqua](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Antoine_Fuqua),[^cite_note-589] [Mayim Bialik](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mayim_Bialik), [Liev Schreiber](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Liev_Schreiber), [Amy Schumer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amy_Schumer), [Michael Douglas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Michael_Douglas), and [Jerry Seinfeld](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jerry_Seinfeld) were among the more than 700 celebrities including [Hollywood](418334.md) professionals who signed an open letter on 12 October condemning Hamas and demanding the safe return of hostages held in Gaza.[^cite_note-variety-open_letter-590] The letter read: "As Israel takes the necessary steps to defend its citizens in the coming days and weeks, social media will be overrun by an orchestrated misinformation campaign spearheaded by Iran. We urge everyone to remember the horrific images that came out of Israel and to not amplify or fall for their propaganda."[^cite_note-591]

Several hundred Hollywood figures including [Jordan Peele](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jordan_Peele), [David Oyelowo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/David_Oyelowo), [Chris Rock](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chris_Rock), [Taika Waititi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Taika_Waititi), [Ewan McGregor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ewan_McGregor), [James Corden](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Corden), [Matthew Weiner](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Matthew_Weiner), [Aaron Sorkin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aaron_Sorkin), [Judd Apatow](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Judd_Apatow), [Greg Berlanti](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Greg_Berlanti), [Bradley Cooper](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bradley_Cooper), [Eugene Levy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eugene_Levy), [Jon Hamm](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jon_Hamm), [Will Ferrell](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Will_Ferrell), [Constance Wu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Constance_Wu), [Jeff Goldblum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeff_Goldblum),[^cite_note-\:15-592] [Bob Odenkirk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bob_Odenkirk), and [Tiffany Haddish](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tiffany_Haddish) signed a letter on 23 October urging the release of the hostages and thanking US President Biden for his leadership in the Gaza war.[^cite_note-593] The letter read: "Thank you for your unshakable moral conviction, leadership and support for the Jewish people, who have been terrorized by Hamas since the group's founding over 35 years ago, and for the Palestinians, who have also been terrorized, oppressed and victimized by Hamas for the last 17 years that the group has been governing Gaza."[^cite_note-\:15-592]

[Drake](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Drake_\(musician\)), [Jennifer Lopez](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jennifer_Lopez), [Bella](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bella_Hadid) and [Gigi Hadid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gigi_Hadid), [Michael Stipe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Michael_Stipe), [Cate Blanchett](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cate_Blanchett), [Zayn Malik](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zayn_Malik), [Frank Ocean](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Frank_Ocean), [Rachel McAdams](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rachel_McAdams), [Oscar Isaac](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oscar_Isaac), [Bradley Cooper](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bradley_Cooper), [David Oyelowo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/David_Oyelowo), [Ben Affleck](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ben_Affleck), [John Cusack](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_Cusack), [Kristen Stewart](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kristen_Stewart), [Channing Tatum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Channing_Tatum), [Quinta Brunson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Quinta_Brunson), [Sandra Oh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sandra_Oh), and [Joaquin Phoenix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Joaquin_Phoenix), among many others, as part of a group called Artists4Ceasefire, signed a letter urging President Joe Biden and Congress to call for an Gere](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Richard_Gere), [Peter Gabriel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Peter_Gabriel), [Viggo Mortensen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Viggo_Mortensen), [Kirsten Dunst](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kirsten_Dunst), [Annie Lennox](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Annie_Lennox), [Alyssa Milano](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alyssa_Milano), [Jim Jarmusch](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jim_Jarmusch), [Brian Cox](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brian_Cox_\(actor\)), [Michael Moore](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Michael_Moore), [Andrew In November 2023, more than 4000 musicians, including [Zack de la Rocha](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zack_de_la_Rocha), [Tom Morello](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tom_Morello), [Bikini Kill](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bikini_Kill), [Pulp](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pulp_\(band\)), [Lucy Dacus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lucy_Dacus), Billy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Will_Oldham), [Keiyaa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Keiyaa), [Pink Siifu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pink_Siifu), [Laurel Halo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Laurel_Halo), [Mykki Blanco](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mykki_Blanco), and [L'Rain](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/L'Rain).[^cite_note-599][^cite_note-600] In Dreijer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Karin_Dreijer), [First Aid Kit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/First_Aid_Kit_\(band\)), [Eric Saade](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eric_Saade) and [Malena Ernman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Malena_Ernman),[^cite_note-604] signed an open letter calling for Israel to be excluded from the 2024 Eurovision Song Contest.[^cite_note-605]

## Religious organizations

### Christian



[United Church of Christ](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Church_of_Christ) altar

-  The [Holy See](13393.md) called for an immediate ceasefire and the release of Israeli hostages, and condemned Hamas' attacks while stating that criminal responsibility for terrorist acts "can never be attributed to an entire nation or people". It expressed support for a [two-state solution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Two-state_solution) with a special status for Jerusalem.

  [^cite_note-606]

- The [World Council of Churches](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_Council_of_Churches), representing the majority of the world's Christian denominations, called for "an immediate cessation of this deadly violence, for Hamas to cease their attacks and ask\[ed] both parties for de-escalation of the situation".

  [^cite_note-Woerman2023-607]

-  The [Eastern Orthodox](10186.md) Ecumenical Patriarch of Constantinople [Bartholomew](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bartholomew_I_of_Constantinople) released a message stating "Our thoughts and prayers are particularly with the civilians... and especially with all the innocent children, who deserve to get raised in a truly "Promised Land" of peace and prosperity".

  [^cite_note-608]

-  The Catholic [Latin Patriarchate of Jerusalem](231524.md) called on the international community and religious leaders "to make every effort in helping to de-escalate the situation, restore calm and work to guarantee the fundamental rights of people in the region" and supported the maintenance of the "Status Quo" with regard to all the Holy Places in the [Holy Land](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Holy_Land), especially in [Jerusalem](16043.md), as well as the "urgent need to find a lasting and comprehensive solution to the Palestinian-Israeli conflict in this land."

  [^cite_note-vatican-380]

-  The Secretary General of the [Anglican Communion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anglican_Communion) Bishop [Anthony Poggo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anthony_Poggo) expressed pain for the people of the region and prayer for the safety of civilians and the cessation of violence. He also offered prayers for Primate [Hosam Naoum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hosam_Naoum) of the [Episcopal Church in Jerusalem and the Middle East](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Episcopal_Church_in_Jerusalem_and_the_Middle_East).

  [^cite_note-609]

- The [Nigerian Christian Pilgrim Commission](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nigerian_Christian_Pilgrim_Commission) announced it was suspending pilgrimages to Israel.

  [^cite_note-610]

- [The First Presidency](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/First_Presidency_\(LDS_Church\)) of [The Church of Jesus Christ of Latter-day Saints](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Church_of_Jesus_Christ_of_Latter-day_Saints) issued a statement about Middle East violence saying it affirmed that God "calls upon all of us to love our neighbors as ourselves, and we pray for a peaceful resolution of all conflicts". LDS President [Russell M. Nelson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russell_M._Nelson) also called for peace.

  [^cite_note-611]

  [^cite_note-612]

- The [National Council of Churches in the Philippines](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Council_of_Churches_in_the_Philippines) called the conflict a "lopsided" war in favor of Israel. It urged Israel's response against Hamas' attack to be scrutinized for violation of international humanitarian law and called for a "just peace".

  [^cite_note-613]

-  On behalf of [Quakerism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Quakers), the [Friends World Committee for Consultation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Friends_World_Committee_for_Consultation) (in collaboration with [QUNO](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Quaker_United_Nations_Office), [FCNL](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Friends_Committee_on_National_Legislation), and [Britain Yearly Meeting](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Britain_Yearly_Meeting), among others) called for "a ceasefire in Israel-Palestine" and emphasized action towards peace. This position references the Quaker [opposition to violence of any kind](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Testimony_of_peace).

  [^cite_note-\:12-614]

  &#x20;The FWCC claimed that Quakers have "been present in the \[Gaza] region for more than 150 years, doing educational, peace, humanitarian and human rights work",

  [^cite_note-\:12-614]

  &#x20;including the [American Friends Service Committee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_Friends_Service_Committee) and [Ramallah Friends School](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ramallah_Friends_School).

  [^cite_note-\:12-614]

### Jewish

- [Ephraim Mirvis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ephraim_Mirvis), the [chief rabbi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chief_Rabbi_of_the_United_Hebrew_Congregations_of_the_Commonwealth) of the [United Kingdom and Commonwealth](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Synagogue), stated "the death of any innocent person is a tragedy," supported Israel to "do whatever it can to guarantee the survival of the state of Israel and the Jewish people," and called Hamas an "existential threat".

  [^cite_note-615]

  &#x20;Mirvis also rejected the accusations of genocide levied against Israel.

  [^cite_note-616]

- [Riccardo Di Segni](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Riccardo_Di_Segni), the chief rabbi of Rome, stated that "Whoever does evil must be defeated, as happened with the Nazis in 1945," and criticized the Catholic and other churches' response for being "a jumble of political and religious declarations that have left us confused and offended ."

  [^cite_note-617]

- In response to a letter by mufti Nazirudin Mohd Nasir of Singapore, chief rabbi Mordechai Abergel called for peaceful coexistence and solidarity.

  [^cite_note-618]

### Muslim

- The [Al-Azhar Al-Sharif](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al-Azhar_Al-Sharif) mosque in [Cairo](6293.md), regarded as [Sunni Islam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sunni_Islam)'s foremost seat of religious learning, issued a statement saying that it stood "fast with full support to the free people of Palestine, who have come to revive our self-confidence, lifeline, and a long-lost sense of aliveness". It also criticised the international community's "double standards when it comes to the Palestinian cause".

  [^cite_note-619]

-  [Muhammadiyah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Muhammadiyah) urged the [UN Security Council](31956.md) "to immediately take political and diplomatic steps involving related parties, especially Israel-Palestine, to stop the war, carry out a ceasefire and carry out peace negotiations" and affirmed its position in supporting the Palestinian cause. It also asked the Indonesian government to be more proactive in resolving the Israeli-Palestinian conflict.

  [^cite_note-ID-620]

-  [Nahdlatul Ulama](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nahdlatul_Ulama) called for the conflict and violence "to be stopped immediately with all efforts" and urged the UN Security Council not to use "the veto right to defend one party in this prolonged humanitarian tragedy".

  [^cite_note-ID-620]

## Student organizations

Main article: [2024 pro-Palestinian protests on university campuses](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_pro-Palestinian_protests_on_university_campuses)



Pro-Palestinian protest outside [Sproul Hall](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sproul_Plaza) at the [University of California, Berkeley](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_California,_Berkeley), 25 October 2023



Pro-Palestinian protesters [occupying the Columbia University campus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Columbia_University_pro-Palestinian_campus_occupation) in April 2024

- A coalition of 34 student organizations from [Harvard University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Harvard_University) issued a statement stating the conflict "did not occur in a vacuum", and that Israel's "[apartheid regime](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israel_and_apartheid) is the only one to blame. Israeli violence has structured every aspect of Palestinian existence for 75 years."

  [^cite_note-621]

  &#x20;The statement was signed by Palestinian and Muslim support groups as well as others including the African American Resistance Organization and the Harvard Jews for Liberation.

  [^cite_note-622]

  &#x20;However several of the organisations such as Amnesty International at Harvard, Harvard College Act on a Dream, the Harvard Undergraduate Nepali Student Association, the Harvard Islamic Society and the Harvard Undergraduate Ghungroo withdrew their support for the statement and condemned the massacres of civilians by Hamas.

  [^cite_note-623]

- The Oxford Palestine Society said in an Instagram post that "the offensive launched from Gaza can only be understood in the context of Israel's ongoing, decades long, military occupation and colonisation of Palestinian land and imposition of a system of oppression that meets the legal definition of apartheid." They also urged students to support the "movement for a free Palestine" and join a scheduled protest on 12 October. In contrast, the Oxford Israel Society said it "unequivocally condemns" the recent conflict and "the massacre and hostage taking committed by Hamas and Islamic Jihad terrorists" and called on [Oxford University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oxford_University) and other colleges to condemn the Palestinian attacks.

  [^cite_note-624]

- [Islami Jamiat Talaba](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islami_Jamiat_Talaba), Pakistan's largest student organization, organized a rally at [Karachi University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Karachi_University) in support of Palestine that was attended by hundreds of students on 10 October, during which a 200-foot [Palestinian flag](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palestinian_flag) was unfurled by the protesters.

  [^cite_note-\:8-625]

- Students at [Cornell University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cornell_University) wrote [spraypainted](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spraypainted) messages related to the conflict on the [Ithaca](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ithaca,_New_York), New York campus, including "Israel is Fascist" and "Free Palestine."

  [^cite_note-626]

  [^cite_note-627]

  &#x20;The slogans were subsequently removed by the university.

  [^cite_note-628]

- On 12 October, a protest at [Columbia University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Columbia_University) in New York City saw the attendance of pro-Palestinian demonstrators.

  [^cite_note-629]

  &#x20;Since November 2023, [Jewish Voice for Peace](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jewish_Voice_for_Peace)'s chapter at Columbia University has been under suspension. The university stated that both the JVP chapter and [Students for Justice in Palestine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Students_for_Justice_in_Palestine) had breached university policies, engaging in "threatening rhetoric and intimidation", leading to the suspension of the clubs.

  [^cite_note-630]

  [^cite_note-631]

- [Al-Azhar University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al-Azhar_University) issued a statement calling "on the free people of the world, international organizations and institutions to take immediate action to break the siege of Gaza hospitals." It also emphasized that silence against the crimes of the occupying-regime is considered a stain on the forehead of humanity and the world community.

  [^cite_note-632]

## Sports organizations



Israeli President [Isaac Herzog](4645588.md) with Israeli athletes in the Olympic village in Paris, 24 July 2024

Palestinian sports organizations and sports organizations from Arab countries have called for sanctions to be imposed against Israel and to prevent its participation in the [2024 Summer Olympics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israel_at_the_2024_Summer_Olympics) due to the Gaza war. The calls from the organizations have been prompted by concerns about the war's impact on Palestinian athletes and sports facilities.[^cite_note-633][^cite_note-634] In November 2023, Russia accused the [International Olympic Committee](15147.md) of having double standards by not sanctioning Israel due to its military actions in Gaza and [occupation of Palestine](2781576.md), as Palestine is also an IOC member.[^cite_note-635] In January 2024, over 300 Palestinian sports clubs called for Israel to be barred from the 2024 Olympics after [Israeli airstrikes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bombing_of_Gaza) had killed Palestine's Olympic football team coach, and damaged the headquarters of the [Palestine Olympic Committee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palestine_Olympic_Committee) in Gaza.[^cite_note-636] In February 2024, several left-wing French lawmakers sent a letter to the IOC proposing to sanction Israeli athletes while only allowing them to participate under the [Individual Neutral Athlete label](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Individual_Neutral_Athletes_at_the_2024_Summer_Olympics).[^cite_note-637] The IOC has cautioned athletes against boycotting or discriminating others, stating that immediate action will follow any discriminatory behavior such as the case of Algerian judoka [Fethi Nourine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fethi_Nourine), who received a ten-year ban following his refusal to fight [Tohar Butbul](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tohar_Butbul), an Israeli in [2020](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2020_Summer_Olympics). The IOC also stated that athletes are not to be held accountable for their government's actions.[^cite_note-638] Due to the continuation of the Israeli-Palestinian war, France asked the United States for help in securing the Olympic Games.[^cite_note-639] In March 2024, IOC President [Thomas Bach](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thomas_Bach) made it clear there was no issue regarding Israel participating at the 2024 Summer Olympics and cautioned athletes against boycotts and discrimination.[^cite_note-640]

On 8 October 2023, [UEFA](32332.md) cancelled all of its scheduled football matches in Israel for the next two weeks, including a qualifier for the [Euro 2024](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Euro_2024) tournament between [Israel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israel_national_football_team) and [Switzerland](681862.md) on 12 October. It also canceled the [2025 UEFA European Under-21 Championship qualification](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_UEFA_European_Under-21_Championship_qualification) games between Israel and [Estonia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Estonia_national_football_team) on 12 October and between Israel and [Germany](250204.md) on 17 October, as well as the [2024 UEFA European Under-17 Championship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_UEFA_European_Under-17_Championship) mini tournament on 11–17 October involving Israel, [Belgium](679716.md), [Gibraltar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gibraltar_national_football_team) and [Wales](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wales_national_football_team).[^cite_note-641] UEFA held a moment of silence for all Euro 2024 qualifying matches on 17 October for both the victims of the war in Israel as well as the [Swedish fans that were murdered in Brussels a day prior](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_Brussels_shooting). Israel later played the rest of their Euro 2024 qualification matches in [Hungary](13275.md), while [Maccabi Haifa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maccabi_Haifa_F.C.) played a Europa League match in [Cyprus](5593.md) before heading to [Serbia](29265.md) alongside [Maccabi Tel Aviv](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maccabi_Tel_Aviv_F.C.). [EuroLeague](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/EuroLeague) Basketball also suspended all home games for Israeli clubs, including [Hapoel Tel Aviv B.C.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hapoel_Tel_Aviv_B.C.) and [Maccabi Playtika Tel Aviv](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maccabi_Playtika_Tel_Aviv), with the teams sending its players to [Cyprus](5593.md) and [Greece](12108.md) to possibly resume the season there. All EuroLeague and EuroCup matches held a moment of silence for the victims of the attack.[^cite_note-642][^cite_note-643]

The [National Football League](21211.md), [National Basketball Association](22093.md), [Major League Baseball](38776.md), [National Hockey League](21809.md), and [Major League Soccer](71802.md), amongst several other North American leagues, all released statements condemning the terrorist attacks on Israeli civilians by Hamas while urging for peace for civilians in the region. Many teams in their respective leagues also released their own statements with the same message. However, there was criticism towards all the leagues and teams for not releasing statements for the Palestinian civilians that had also been killed in the conflict.[^cite_note-644][^cite_note-645][^cite_note-646] All NFL teams during Week 6 held a moment of silence for the Israeli and American victims as well as those affected by the conflict, starting with a Thursday Night game between the [Denver Broncos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Denver_Broncos) and [Kansas City Chiefs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kansas_City_Chiefs).[^cite_note-647] However, during the London game between the [Baltimore Ravens](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Baltimore_Ravens) and [Tennessee Titans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tennessee_Titans), the moment of silence was interrupted by some spectators chanting "Free Palestine".

In the United Kingdom, there was pressure for the [English FA](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Football_Association) and the [Premier League](11250.md) to release statements and honour the victims of the attacks by Hamas, with some noting that other tragedies, disasters, and social movements had been honoured much more quickly.[^cite_note-648][^cite_note-649] Eventually the English FA honoured both the Israeli and Palestinian victims at a friendly with [Australia](4792249.md) and a [UEFA Euro 2024 qualifying match](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UEFA_Euro_2024_qualifying_Group_C) with [Italy](362466.md). However, they did not light up [Wembley Stadium](8913012.md) in the colours of the Israeli flag, believed to be due to one of Australia's 2026 FIFA World Cup qualifier opponents being [Palestine](1302455.md).[^cite_note-650][^cite_note-651] The Premier League also honoured the victims following the international break.[^cite_note-652] The FA has also banned the use of the phrase "[from the river to the sea](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/From_the_river_to_the_sea)" on any players' social media following its use by [Hamza Choudhury](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hamza_Choudhury).[^cite_note-653]

Following a request from the [Palestinian Football Association](5531531.md)'s head [Jibril Rajoub](26480696.md), the [Algerian Football Federation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Algerian_Football_Federation) offered to host "all official and non-official matches" of the Palestinian national team as part of its preparations for its qualifiers for the [2026 World Cup and 2027 Asian Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2026_FIFA_World_Cup_qualification_%E2%80%93_AFC_second_round). It also pledged to shoulder all associated costs.[^cite_note-654] The PFA later accepted Algeria's request, though after FIFA and the AFC prevented the team from playing in Algeria, they selected [Kuwait](7515890.md), [Qatar](8238258.md), and other countries in the [Islamic world](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islamic_world) instead. [Lebanon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lebanon_national_football_team), Palestine and Israel's neighbour to the north, were also forced to play the same 2026 World Cup and 2027 Asian Cup qualification in neutral ground due to the fear of the war's spillover to the country.[^cite_note-655] [Celtic F.C.](69708.md) was fined 17,500 euros (\$19,000) by UEFA after fans displayed a flag of the [Popular Front for the Liberation of Palestine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Popular_Front_for_the_Liberation_of_Palestine) during a [Champions League](44220.md) match against [Atletico Madrid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Atletico_Madrid) on 25 October.[^cite_note-656] A number of people protested at the Paris Organizing Committee for the Olympic and Paralympic Games headquarters in the suburb of [Saint-Denis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saint-Denis,_Seine-Saint-Denis), waving Palestinian flags and chanting slogans condemning Israel's "institutional involvement" in the Games as a result of the ongoing conflict in Gaza. The demonstrators urged Olympic authorities to restrict Israel's involvement in the Paris Games.[^cite_note-657]

The left-wing [Podemos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Podemos_\(Spanish_political_party\)) party in [Spain](26667.md) requested the exclusion of Israel from the Olympics and called on countries to boycott the competition if Israeli continued to participate.[^cite_note-658][^cite_note-659] Ione Belarra, the Podemos party's secretary general and former minister of social rights in Spain, called for Israel to be removed from the Olympics.[^cite_note-660] During the 74th [FIFA Congress](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FIFA_Congress) on 17 May 2024 in [Bangkok](56615.md), the [Palestinian Football Association](5531531.md) called for a vote on suspending the IFA from both UEFA and FIFA, stating that the damage to football infrastructure and deaths of Palestinian footballers during the [Gaza war](74998519.md) were enough grounds to justify the action, while other supporters referred to other suspensions such as those of [Apartheid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Apartheid) [South Africa](17416221.md) from 1961 to 1991 and [Russia](25391.md) following the [Russian invasion of Ukraine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russian_invasion_of_Ukraine) in 2022. The IFA responded stating that their football was halted and that many of their footballers have been killed since [7 October 2023](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_Hamas-led_attack_on_Israel) while calling for peace and a future friendly between the two countries. [Gianni Infantino](9286117.md) would respond to the PFA stating that FIFA would conduct its own legal review and that the information about a final decision would be forwarded to the FIFA Council meeting on 3 October 2024 while also calling for peace.[^cite_note-661][^cite_note-662] It was later decided that while Israel would not be suspended, FIFA would investigate the claims by the PFA.[^cite_note-663] Prior to [qualification](49459509.md) for the [2026 FIFA World Cup](36581929.md), the [Norwegian Football Federation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Norwegian_Football_Federation) called for an investigation into Israel (who they were drawn against), stating that they wanted to end civilian casualties in the region.[^cite_note-664]

### Individual athletes

[Bayern Munich](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bayern_Munich) and [Morocco](1024865.md) defender [Noussair Mazraoui](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Noussair_Mazraoui) posted in support of Palestine on social media.[^cite_note-665] [Liverpool](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Liverpool_F.C.) and [Egypt](1022953.md) captain [Mohamed Salah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mohamed_Salah) appealed for humanitarian aid to be allowed into Gaza and for the "massacres" and the "slaughter of innocent civilians" to stop;[^cite_note-666] [Al-Ittihad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al-Ittihad_Club_\(Jeddah\))'s French forward [Karim Benzema](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Karim_Benzema) called the bombing of Gaza "unjust". In response, French interior minister [Gerald Darmanin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gerald_Darmanin) accused him of having links to the [Muslim Brotherhood](20742.md).[^cite_note-667]

[OGC Nice](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/OGC_Nice) suspended its Algerian defender [Youcef Atal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Youcef_Atal) after he shared a video on Instagram from a Palestinian preacher purportedly calling for violence against Jewish people.[^cite_note-668] He was later detained by French authorities[^cite_note-669] and received an eight-month suspended sentence from a French court on charges of inciting hatred.[^cite_note-670] [1. FSV Mainz 05](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1._FSV_Mainz_05) also suspended and later dismissed its Dutch forward [Anwar El Ghazi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anwar_El_Ghazi) after he made what the club called an "unacceptable" social media post about the war.[^cite_note-671][^cite_note-672]

Following the [al-Ahli Arab Hospital explosion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al-Ahli_Arab_Hospital_explosion), [Tottenham Hotspur](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tottenham_Hotspur)'s Israeli winger [Manor Solomon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Manor_Solomon) purportedly blamed Palestinians for "killing their own people and blaming Israel" in an Instagram post.[^cite_note-673] [Granada](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Granada_CF)'s Israeli forward [Shon Weissman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shon_Weissman) posted social media messages in support of his country following Hamas' attack. After receiving threats in response, he was excluded from a [La Liga](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/La_Liga) match against [Osasuna](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Osasuna) on 20 October due to security issues.[^cite_note-674] Muslim American basketball player [Enes Kanter](22346872.md) said the Hamas attack was a "barbaric" act of terrorism that did not reflect Islamic teachings.[^cite_note-675] On 12 November, he said anti-Israel protesters on college campuses were "brainwashed" and urged people not to get their information from [TikTok](56822861.md).[^cite_note-676][^cite_note-677]

Egyptian swimmer [Abdelrahman Sameh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abdelrahman_Sameh) and Tunisian Olympic swimmer [Ahmed Hafnaoui](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ahmed_Hafnaoui) were both criticized and subjected to death threats due to their support for Palestine on social media, with American swimmer [Eli Cohen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eli_Cohen) telling Hafnaoui he was asking people to "donate to terror" after the latter posted a link to a humanitarian fundraiser for war victims. Likewise, [Israel Swimming Association](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israel_Swimming_Association) Chairman [Miki Halika](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Miki_Halika) wrote to the sport's international governing body [World Aquatics](317029.md) urging it to "take action against swimmers who support terrorism".[^cite_note-678] Mixed-martial arts champion [Khabib Nurmagomedov](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Khabib_Nurmagomedov) posted on Instagram that Israel was engaged in "genocide" in Gaza.[^cite_note-679]

NBA star [LeBron James](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/LeBron_James), XFL co-owner [Dwayne Johnson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dwayne_Johnson), former NFL players [Julian Edelman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Julian_Edelman) and [Tom Brady](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tom_Brady), and former NBA player [Amar'e Stoudemire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amar'e_Stoudemire) all made posts supporting peace in Israel. Israeli–American [Baltimore Orioles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Baltimore_Orioles) pitcher [Dean Kremer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dean_Kremer) wore a [Star of David](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Star_of_David) during a playoff game against the [Texas Rangers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Texas_Rangers_\(baseball\)), as did [Houston Astros](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Houston_Astros) shortstop [Alex Bregman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alex_Bregman). Israeli right-winger [Sagiv Yehezkel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sagiv_Yehezkel) was suspended from [Antalyaspor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Antalyaspor) and later detained by Turkish authorities after showing his bandaged wrist during a match against [Trabzonspor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Trabzonspor) with the message "100 days, 7/10" in support of hostages in Gaza on 14 January 2024, which Turkish Justice Minister [Yılmaz Tunç](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Y%C4%B1lmaz_Tun%C3%A7) claimed was an "ugly action supporting Israel's massacre in Gaza".[^cite_note-680] [Başakşehir](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/%C4%B0stanbul_Ba%C5%9Fak%C5%9Fehir_F.K.) ended its contract with Israeli player [Eden Karzev](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eden_Karzev) after he re-posted a social media message with the hashtag "BringThemHomeNow", referring to the hostages held by Hamas.[^cite_note-681]

## Other organizations



Pro-Palestinian protest during the opening ceremony of the [Eurovision Song Contest](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eurovision_Song_Contest_2025) in [Basel](4911.md), Switzerland, 11 May 2025

On 16 February 2024 a coalition of several U.S. unions announced a call for a ceasefire. The unions claim to represent some nine million teachers, nurses, auto workers,[^cite_note-682] flight attendants and others.[^cite_note-683]

### Eurovision Song Contest 2024

The [ongoing humanitarian crisis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_Gaza_humanitarian_crisis) resulting from the Israeli retaliatory operations in the Gaza Strip since October 2023 led to increasing calls that the [European Broadcasting Union](10380.md) (EBU) exclude Israel from taking part in the [Eurovision Song Contest 2024](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eurovision_Song_Contest_2024), with human rights activists urging other countries to boycott the event.[^cite_note-684] Despite this, Israel ultimately appeared on the list of participants released by the EBU on 5 December 2023.[^cite_note-685][^cite_note-686] The EBU said in a statement: "It is a competition for broadcasters (not governments) and the [Israeli public broadcaster](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israeli_Public_Broadcasting_Corporation) has participated in the Contest for 50 years. The Eurovision Song Contest remains a non-political event that unites audiences worldwide through music."[^cite_note-687]

The Icelandic Association of Composers and Lyricists (FTT), which represents artists in [Iceland](14531.md), told its members in a statement to not participate in the [Eurovision Song Contest 2024](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israel_in_the_Eurovision_Song_Contest_2024) unless Israel is removed from the competition, on the same grounds as [Russia](25391.md) was in the last competition, due to Israel's conduct in the Gaza war.[^cite_note-688] FTT also urged the [Icelandic National Broadcaster](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/R%C3%9AV) (RÚV) to withdraw from the competition unless Israel is denied participation on the same grounds as Russia was due to its [invasion of Ukraine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russian_invasion_of_Ukraine).[^cite_note-689]

### Transatlantic Civil Servants' Statement on Gaza

The Transatlantic Civil Servants' Statement on Gaza is a public letter signed by over 800 currently serving officials in the United States and Europe which states that the policies of their governments during the [Israel-Gaza war](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israel-Gaza_war) could amount to "grave violations of international law". The signatories are civil servants from the United States, the European Union, and 11 European countries including France, Germany, and the United Kingdom. The identities of the signers were not made public for fear of repercussions.[^cite_note-690][^cite_note-691][^cite_note-692][^cite_note-693][^cite_note-694]

## Visits by world leaders

Numerous heads of state and government have visited Israel, [Ramallah](44132.md), and Egypt for consultations.

- Olaf Scholz and Isaac Herzog, 17 October 2023

  [Olaf Scholz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Olaf_Scholz) and [Isaac Herzog](4645588.md), 17 October 2023

- Joe Biden and Benjamin Netanyahu, 18 October 2023

  [Joe Biden](145422.md) and [Benjamin Netanyahu](56472.md), 18 October 2023

- Rishi Sunak and Mahmoud Abbas, 20 October 2023

  [Rishi Sunak](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rishi_Sunak) and [Mahmoud Abbas](232595.md), 20 October 2023

- Emmanuel Macron and Isaac Herzog, 24 October 2023

  [Emmanuel Macron](43671127.md) and Isaac Herzog, 24 October 2023

- Edgars Rinkēvičs and Isaac Herzog, 20 November 2023

  [Edgars Rinkēvičs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Edgars_Rink%C4%93vi%C4%8Ds) and Isaac Herzog, 20 November 2023

- Pedro Sánchez, Mahmoud Abbas, and Alexander De Croo, 23 November 2023

  [Pedro Sánchez](43297512.md), Mahmoud Abbas, and [Alexander De Croo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alexander_De_Croo), 23 November 2023

- Javier Milei and Isaac Herzog, 6 February 2024

  [Javier Milei](56599840.md) and Isaac Herzog, 6 February 2024

European Commission President [Ursula von der Leyen](2991932.md) and European Parliament President [Roberta Metsola](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roberta_Metsola) arrived in Israel on 13 October.[^cite_note-695] On 17 October, German Chancellor [Olaf Scholz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Olaf_Scholz) visited Israel to express solidarity with the country. On his departure from Ben-Gurion airport, he was evacuated to a shelter after a rocket alarm went off.[^cite_note-696] On 18 October, US President [Joe Biden](145422.md) arrived in Israel and was received at Ben-Gurion airport by Israeli President Isaac Herzog and Prime Minister Benjamin Netanyahu. At a news conference, he said Israel did not commit the al-Ahli hospital bombing in Gaza and blamed what he called "the other team" for the attack.[^cite_note-697] In the wake of the attack, a summit in [Amman](45007.md) hosted by King Abdullah II that was also to be attended by Biden, Palestinian President Mahmoud Abbas and Egyptian President Abdel Fattah el Sisi was cancelled by the Jordanian government.[^cite_note-698]

British Prime Minister [Rishi Sunak](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rishi_Sunak) arrived in Israel on 19 October, in order to meet Netanyahu and offer his condolences for the civilians killed in the initial attacks.[^cite_note-699] On 21 October 2023, Italian Prime Minister [Giorgia Meloni](29228976.md) visited Israel to express solidarity with the country.[^cite_note-700] French President [Emmanuel Macron](43671127.md) arrived in Israel on 24 October.[^cite_note-701] Czech Prime Minister [Petr Fiala](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Petr_Fiala) arrived in Israel on 25 October.[^cite_note-Haaretz-702] During the visit of Spanish Prime Minister [Pedro Sánchez](43297512.md) and Belgian Prime Minister [Alexander De Croo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alexander_De_Croo), Netanyahu was asked to respect international law, and Sánchez announced that Spain was ready to recognize the State of Palestine.[^cite_note-Jones-703] This triggered a harsh reaction from Israeli foreign minister [Eli Cohen](46195208.md), who subsequently accused both prime ministers of "giving support to terrorism" in Israel.[^cite_note-704] On 6 February 2024, Argentine president [Javier Milei](56599840.md) became the first Latin American president to visit Israel since the start of the war, where he condemned Hamas attacks and confirmed his position of moving the Argentine embassy to Jerusalem.[^cite_note-MileiinIsrael1-705][^cite_note-MileiinIsrael-706]
