| [Ecuador](9334.md) Ecuador | [President](840787.md) | [Daniel Noboa](73980059.md) | 4–6 May 2025 | [^cite_note-726] |
| [Argentina](18951905.md) Argentina | President | Javier Milei | 11 June 2025 | [^cite_note-727] |
| [Fiji](10707.md) Fiji | Prime Minister | [Sitiveni Rabuka](516580.md) | 17 September 2025 | [^cite_note-728] |

## Demonstrations

Main article: [Gaza war protests](75044900.md)

Demonstrations took place in many Middle Eastern countries, such as Yemen, Jordan,[^cite_note-729] Lebanon, Kuwait, Turkey, Qatar,[^cite_note-730] Bahrain, Syria, Egypt,[^cite_note-731] Iraq[^cite_note-\:5-732] and Iran in solidarity with the Palestinian groups.[^cite_note-ajlive3-733][^cite_note-734] There were reports that many Iranians avoided these demonstrations and condemned the attacks online.[^cite_note-735][^cite_note-736][^cite_note-737] Pro-Palestinian rallies were also held in Australia,[^cite_note-738] Spain,[^cite_note-739] South Africa,[^cite_note-ajlive3-733] Pakistan,[^cite_note-\:8-625] Libya, Tunisia,[^cite_note-740] Morocco, Bangladesh, India, Sri Lanka,[^cite_note-\:9-741] Serbia,[^cite_note-742] Greece,[^cite_note-743] Chile, Belgium, Italy,[^cite_note-744] Philippines,[^cite_note-745] South Korea, Ireland,[^cite_note-746] Bosnia and Herzegovina, Bolivia, Sweden, and Denmark.[^cite_note-747]

- Anti-war demonstrations in Amsterdam on 15 October

  Anti-war demonstrations in [Amsterdam](844.md) on 15 October

- Pro-Israeli protest in Berlin on 8 October

  Pro-Israeli protest in [Berlin](3354.md) on 8 October

- Pro-Palestinian protest in London on 9 October

  Pro-Palestinian protest in [London](17867.md) on 9 October

- Pro-Palestinian protest in Bangladesh on 13 October

  Pro-Palestinian protest in [Bangladesh](3454.md) on 13 October

- Pro-Palestine protest in Melbourne on 14 October

  Pro-Palestine protest in [Melbourne](17306237.md) on 14 October

- Pro-Palestine rally in Toronto on 28 October

  Pro-Palestine rally in [Toronto](64646.md) on 28 October

Rallies in support of both Israel and Palestine were held across Canada,[^cite_note-748][^cite_note-749] as well as the United States.[^cite_note-US-750][^cite_note-751][^cite_note-752] On 9 October, German President [Frank-Walter Steinmeier](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Frank-Walter_Steinmeier) said that he would not tolerate public celebrations of the Hamas attacks. The German [Green Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alliance_90/The_Greens) asked for restrictions on organizations which openly support Hamas. The liberal conservative [Christian Democratic Union of Germany](60584.md) asked for those who supported Hamas to have their German citizenship revoked. The [Central Council of Jews in Germany](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Central_Council_of_Jews_in_Germany) and the Israeli ambassador both asked for actions to be taken.[^cite_note-753] Chancellor [Olaf Scholz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Olaf_Scholz) announced plans to ban the pro-Palestinian group [Samidoun](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Samidoun) after it was pictured handing out sweets in Berlin to celebrate Hamas' attack on 7 October.[^cite_note-754][^cite_note-bbcfrance-558] Police subsequently banned pro-Palestine demonstrations in Berlin, while French police arrested ten people in rallies in [Lille](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lille) and in Paris' [Place de la République](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Place_de_la_R%C3%A9publique) after the government issued a similar ban.[^cite_note-755][^cite_note-756] [Amnesty International](18947898.md) called on European countries to safeguard the right to protest, saying that many countries "are unlawfully restricting the right to protest".[^cite_note-757]

In the United Kingdom, Foreign Secretary [James Cleverly](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Cleverly) urged Palestine supporters to stay home, saying that their protests were causing concern in the [Jewish community](5380456.md).[^cite_note-758] Citing laws prohibiting explicit support for banned organisations, authorities arrested a woman in [Brighton](49796.md) for making a speech in support of Hamas at a protest.[^cite_note-759] One rally in London drew an estimated 100,000 protestors in support of the Palestinians.[^cite_note-760] 60,000 people later marched in London to protest against a rise in antisemitism from the war.[^cite_note-761][^cite_note-762] At an October rally in [Dublin](8504.md) Ireland, former justice minister [Alan Shatter](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alan_Shatter) voiced support for Israel and denounced "the [third Reich](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Third_Reich) on the streets of Dublin."[^cite_note-763] Several landmarks, such as [Taipei 101](203801.md),[^cite_note-764] the [Sydney Opera House](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sydney_Opera_House),[^cite_note-opera-765] the [Brandenburg Gate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brandenburg_Gate), the [White House](33057.md), and the [Eiffel Tower](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eiffel_Tower), were lit up in the colors of the Israeli flag.[^cite_note-landmark-257]

## Boycott of Western companies



Protest outside [Raytheon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/RTX_Corporation)'s office in [Goleta, California](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Goleta,_California), 9 November

See also: [List of companies involved in the Gaza war](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_companies_involved_in_the_Gaza_war); [Boycott, Divestment and Sanctions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Boycott,_Divestment_and_Sanctions); and [Boycotts of Israel](43473050.md)

People in some [Muslim](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Muslim_world) countries have boycotted Western businesses, especially American ones such as [Starbucks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Starbucks), [McDonald's](2480627.md) and [Coca-Cola](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coca-Cola), reflecting anger at Western governments' support for Israel.[^cite_note-766][^cite_note-767][^cite_note-768][^cite_note-769][^cite_note-770] Campaigners in [Derry](9055.md), Northern Ireland persuaded [O'Neills](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/O'Neills) to remove [Puma](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Puma_\(brand\)) products because of the sports brand's sponsoring of the [Israeli Football Association](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israeli_Football_Association).[^cite_note-771] Starbucks was targeted in [Keighley](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Keighley), [West Yorkshire](149139.md), with protesters smashing the shop's windows, following the corporation's decision to sue the [Starbuck Workers United](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Starbucks_unions#Starbucks_Workers_United) union after the labour organisation posted on social media support for Palestine.[^cite_note-772]

[Direct action](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Direct_action) was taken at [arms factories](608296.md) in the United States and the United Kingdom that supplied arms to Israel, such as [Lockheed Martin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lockheed_Martin),[^cite_note-773][^cite_note-774] [General Dynamics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/General_Dynamics),[^cite_note-775] [Textron](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Textron),[^cite_note-776] [Boeing](18933266.md),[^cite_note-777] [Raytheon Technologies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/RTX_Corporation) and [Northrop Grumman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Northrop_Grumman).[^cite_note-778] For instance, on 10 November 2023, trade unionists in [Rochester](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rochester,_Kent), Kent, blocked the entrances to a [BAE Systems](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BAE_Systems) factory, stating the facility manufactured [military aircraft](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Military_aircraft) components used to bomb Gaza;[^cite_note-779] and on 16 November, [Palestine Action](67777543.md) occupied a [Leonardo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Leonardo_S.p.A.) factory in [Southampton](7920751.md), stopping production.[^cite_note-780] [ZIM](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ZIM_\(shipping_company\)) was barred from entering ports in Malaysia.[^cite_note-781]

Student protesters called on [Columbia University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Columbia_University) to financially divest from any company with business ties to the Israeli government, including [Microsoft](19001.md), [Google](1092923.md) and [Amazon](90451.md).[^cite_note-782] NYU Alumni for Palestine called on [New York University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_York_University) to "terminate all vendor contracts with companies playing active roles in the military occupation in Palestine and ongoing genocide in Gaza, namely [Cisco](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cisco), Lockheed Martin, [Caterpillar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Caterpillar_Inc.) and [General Electric](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/General_Electric)".[^cite_note-783]

## Weapons transfers

See also: [List of military aid to Israel during the Gaza war](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_military_aid_to_Israel_during_the_Gaza_war), [Arms embargoes on Israel since 2023](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arms_embargoes_on_Israel_since_2023), and [Israel–United States military relations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israel%E2%80%93United_States_military_relations)



US Secretary of Defense [Lloyd Austin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lloyd_Austin), Israeli Defense Minister [Yoav Gallant](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yoav_Gallant), US Joint Chiefs of Staff Chairman [Charles Q. Brown Jr.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Charles_Q._Brown_Jr.) and Israeli Chief of General Staff [Herzi Halevi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Herzi_Halevi) in Tel Aviv, Israel, 18 December 2023

President Biden called on Congress to pass \$14.3 billion in emergency military aid to Israel.[^cite_note-784] The Biden Administration caused controversy after bypassing Congress on multiple occasions to authorize arms sales to the Israeli military.[^cite_note-785]

In December 2023, a *[Wall Street Journal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wall_Street_Journal)* report stated that US arms shipments to Israel since the start of the war included 15,000 bombs and 57,000 [155mm artillery shells](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/155_mm_caliber), mostly carried on C-17 military cargo planes. U.S. has also sent more than 5,000 unguided [Mk82 bombs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mark_82_bomb), more than 5,400 [Mk84 bombs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mark_84_bomb), about 1,000 small diameter [GBU-39 bombs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/GBU-39_Small_Diameter_Bomb), and almost 3,000 JDAMs. It also said that some of the bloodiest Israeli attacks on the Gaza Strip involved the use of large US-made bombs, such as the one that destroyed an apartment complex in the [Jabalia refugee camp](18169472.md) and killed over a hundred people.[^cite_note-786]

The United Kingdom, Germany, France, Canada, Australia, Spain, the Netherlands and Italy also supplied arms to Israel.[^cite_note-euronews-arms-284][^cite_note-787][^cite_note-788][^cite_note-789] On 29 February 2024, the [European Parliament](9581.md) overwhelmingly rejected an arms embargo on Israel.[^cite_note-790] In May 2024, a group of 300 Australian public servants called on the government to cease weapons transfers to Israel.[^cite_note-791] Serbia's state-owned arms manufacturer, [Yugoimport SDPR](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yugoimport_SDPR) exported weapons worth around \$17.1 million to Israel, using IDF planes and civilian aircraft.[^cite_note-792][^cite_note-793]

## Evacuations of foreign nationals

Main article: [Evacuations during the Gaza war](75039684.md)



Australia-assisted departures from the West Bank in October 2023

On 8 October, the Brazilian government announced a rescue operation of Brazilian nationals using an [Airbus A330](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Airbus_A330) of the [Brazilian Air Force](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brazilian_Air_Force), accompanied by doctors and psychologists.[^cite_note-794] Thai Prime Minister Srettha Thavisin put the [Royal Thai Air Force](1778758.md) on standby to evacuate its citizens if needed.[^cite_note-auto-224] Polish Defence Minister [Mariusz Błaszczak](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mariusz_B%C5%82aszczak) announced that two [C-130](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/C-130) transport planes would be deployed to evacuate 200 of its nationals from Israel.[^cite_note-f24live2-795] Hungary evacuated 215 of its nationals from Israel using two aircraft on 9 October, while Romania evacuated 245 of its citizens, including two pilgrimage groups, on two [TAROM](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/TAROM) planes and two private aircraft on the same day.[^cite_note-796] Australia also announced repatriation flights.[^cite_note-797] 300 Nigerian pilgrims in Israel fled to Jordan before being airlifted home.[^cite_note-798]

On 12 October, the United Kingdom arranged flights for its citizens in Israel; the first plane departed [Ben-Gurion Airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ben-Gurion_Airport) that day. The government had said before that it would not be evacuating its nationals due to available commercial flights. However, the flights were commercial.[^cite_note-799] Nepal also arranged a [Nepal Airlines](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nepal_Airlines) flight to repatriate 253 of its citizens back home.[^cite_note-800]

By 16 October, New Zealand had evacuated 55 of its citizens through mercy flights organised by the [Ministry of Foreign Affairs and Trade](5982589.md) and [Etihad Airlines](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Etihad_Airlines). Evacuees were flown to [Abu Dhabi](18950756.md), where they were expected to make their own travel arrangements home. 70 New Zealanders remained behind, with 50 in Israel and 20 in the Palestinian Territories. The Foreign Ministry said that commercial options were available for them.[^cite_note-801] India launched [Operation Ajay](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Operation_Ajay) to repatriate people, mostly Indian nationals in Israel. 1,192 people have been repatriated as of 17 October.[^cite_note-802]

## Opinion polling

### Europe

In Britain, a [YouGov](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/YouGov) poll conducted on 19 October found that 76% of respondents supported a ceasefire in the Gaza war.[^cite_note-803] An [Ipsos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ipsos) poll carried out on 20–23 October, found that 37% of British respondents wanted the UK government to be a neutral mediator in the conflict, 16% wanted Britain to stay out of the conflict at all, 13% would support Israel and 12% would support Palestine.[^cite_note-804] According to a YouGov poll conducted on 7–8 November 2023, older people and [Conservatives](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Conservative_Party_\(UK\)) are more likely to sympathize with the Israelis, while younger people and [Labour Party](19279158.md) voters are more likely to sympathize with the Palestinians.[^cite_note-805]

According to a poll conducted by the [Forsa Institute](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Forsa_Institute) on behalf of newspaper *[Die Welt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Die_Welt)* in December 2023, 45% of respondents in [Germany](11867.md) agreed and 43% disagreed with the statement "Israel's military action in the Gaza Strip is all in all appropriate". In the immediate aftermath of the Hamas attack on Israel, 44% of Germans said Germany has "a special obligation towards Israel". In December 2023, that number dropped to 37%.[^cite_note-806]

A survey commissioned by [Baltic News Service](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Baltic_News_Service) in November found that 38.1% of respondents in [Lithuania](17675.md) supported Israel's actions in Gaza, 30.4% did not support and 31.5% had no opinion.[^cite_note-807] Voters of the [Homeland Union-Lithuanian Christian Democrats](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Homeland_Union-Lithuanian_Christian_Democrats) (58.8%), [Union of Democrats "For Lithuania"](70139653.md), [Liberals' Movement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Liberals'_Movement_\(Lithuania\)) and [Freedom Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Freedom_Party_\(Lithuania\)) were most in favor of Israel's actions, while voters of [Lithuanian Regions Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lithuanian_Regions_Party) and [People and Justice Union](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/People_and_Justice_Union) were least in favor.[^cite_note-808]

According to a survey commissioned by the *[Blick](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Blick)* newspaper on 10–15 November, 72% of Swiss respondents believed Israel had a right to defend itself and reply militarily in Gaza while 67% felt Switzerland should temporarily suspend financial aid to the Palestinian occupied territories. At the same time, 58% said Switzerland's approval of the UN resolution for a ceasefire was "fair" or "quite fair".[^cite_note-809]

A survey conducted by the [Levada Center](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Levada_Center) from 19 to 25 October 2023 found that 66% of Russian respondents expressed a neutral position on the conflict, 21% sympathized with the Palestinians, and 6% sympathized with Israel. 45% of respondents blamed the conflict's escalation on the United States and [NATO](21133.md) (a view promoted by Russian state-controlled media and President [Vladimir Putin](32817.md)), while 12% blamed Israel and 8% blamed Hamas.[^cite_note-810][^cite_note-811]

A survey conducted by the [Kyiv International Institute of Sociology](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kyiv_International_Institute_of_Sociology) from 29 November to 9 December 2023 had 69% of Ukrainian respondents sympathising with Israel compared to only 1% sympathising with Palestine.[^cite_note-812][^cite_note-813]

### Middle East

In [Turkey](11125639.md), a MetroPoll survey released on 25 October found that 34.5% of respondents wanted the government to remain neutral, 26.4% wanted the government to mediate, 18% wanted it express solidarity with the Palestinians while distancing itself from Hamas, and 11.3% wanted it to stand with Hamas.[^cite_note-814][^cite_note-815]

According to a poll conducted from 31 January to 7 February 2024 by the Group for Analyzing and Measuring Attitudes in Iran (GAMMAN), about 35% of Iranians blame Hamas for the war, 20% blame Israel, and 32% blame both sides.[^cite_note-816]

According to the poll conducted by [The Washington Institute for Near East Policy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Washington_Institute_for_Near_East_Policy) between 14 November and 6 December 2023, 96% of respondents in [Saudi Arabia](349303.md) believed that Arab nations should sever relations with Israel, 95% of Saudis did not believe that Hamas killed civilians in its attacks on Israel, and 40% of Saudis expressed a positive view of Hamas.[^cite_note-817]

### East Asia

An 24 October survey in [Taiwan](25734.md) from the [Taiwanese Public Opinion Foundation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Taiwanese_Public_Opinion_Foundation?action=edit\&redlink=1) had 35.1% of respondents sympathising with Israel, 14.8% sympathising with Palestine, 9.8% sympathising with neither, and 33.6% having no opinion.[^cite_note-818][^cite_note-819]

### North America

In November 2023, 59% of Canadians approved of the country's support for Israel in the Gaza war, while 18% disapproved.[^cite_note-820]

The [Marist Poll](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marist_Poll) published on 15 November found that 38% of Americans believe Israel has gone "too far" in its response to Hamas' attack. It also found that American respondents were more likely to sympathize with Israelis (61%) than Palestinians (30%).[^cite_note-821]

A [Quinnipiac](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Quinnipiac_University) study done following war revealed that 76% of American voters believed supporting Israel is in the national interest of the United States, and 64% approved of [sending weapons and military equipment to Israel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israel%E2%80%93United_States_military_relations). This support was more pronounced among older age groups, with 51% of those under 35 disapproving of this action.[^cite_note-822][^cite_note-823] According to a combined [NPR](29697232.md)/*[PBS NewsHour](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/PBS_NewsHour)*/[Marist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marist_Poll) poll, just 48% of [millennials](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Millennials) and members of [Generation Z](4015968.md) think that the US should openly support Israel, compared to 83% of the [Baby boomers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Baby_boomers) and 86% from the [Silent](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Silent_Generation) and [Greatest Generation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Greatest_Generation) (see also [Generation Z and the Israel-Hamas war](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Generation_Z_and_the_Israel-Hamas_war)).[^cite_note-brookings.edu-824][^cite_note-Montanaro-825][^cite_note-Smiththeguardian-826] [Gallup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gallup,_Inc.) polling of [Democrats](5043544.md)' opinion of President Biden's performance dropped by 11% during October 2023, which was attributed to Biden alienating some members of his party with his swift and decisive show of support for Israel.[^cite_note-Smiththeguardian-826] In November 2023, 31% of participants in a survey in the United States expressed their backing for providing weapons to Israel, whereas 43% expressed their opposition to this notion. The remaining respondents remained uncertain about their stance. Notably, Republicans exhibited the highest level of support for sending weapons to Israel, while approximately half of the Democrats were against it.[^cite_note-827]

In November 2023, a Gallup poll found that 50% of respondents in the United States supported Israel's actions in Gaza, while 45% disapproved. 61% of white American respondents expressed support for Israel while only 30% of [people of color](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/People_of_color) did the same.[^cite_note-828] In December 2023, a *New York Times* and Siena College poll found that 44% of Americans believe that Israel should "stop its military campaign", even at the expense of hostages, while 39% believed the opposite. 48% believed that Israel is "not taking enough precautions to avoid civilian casualties", while 30% believed Israel was doing enough.[^cite_note-829] According to the poll conducted by the Harvard CAPS–[Harris Poll](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Harris_Poll) on 17–18 January 2024, 80% of American respondents said they supported Israel more than Hamas in the war.[^cite_note-830] 67% said that a ceasefire in Gaza should only happen after the release of all Israeli hostages and the removal of Hamas from power.[^cite_note-831]

In a *[The Economist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Economist)*/[YouGov](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/YouGov) poll released on 24 January 2024, 35% of U.S. adults agreed that Israel's military campaign against Palestinians [amounts to genocide](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Allegations_of_genocide_in_the_2023_Israeli_attack_on_Gaza), 36% disagreed, and 29% were undecided. Among U.S. citizens aged 18–29, 49% of those surveyed agreed that Israel is committing genocide, with 24% disagreeing, and 27% uncertain.[^cite_note-832] 74% of American respondents also believed that the initial Hamas attack was [genocidal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Allegations_of_genocide_in_the_October_7_Hamas-led_attack_on_Israel).[^cite_note-Montanaro-825] In an [Associated Press](18935732.md)-[NORC Center for Public Affairs Research](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/NORC_Center_for_Public_Affairs_Research) poll released on 2 February 2024, 50% of U.S. adults said that the military response from Israel in the Gaza Strip had gone too far, whereas 31% said that it had "been about right", and 15% thought that it had "not gone far enough".[^cite_note-833] In a [Center for Economic and Policy Research](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Center_for_Economic_and_Policy_Research) poll released on 5 March 2024, 52% of U.S. citizens supported ending arms transfers to Israel. 62% of Americans who voted for Biden in 2020 supported halting arms sales, while 14% disagreed, whereas 30% of Trump voters supported the measure, and 55% opposed it.[^cite_note-CEPRMarch6-834] In a [Gallup Poll](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gallup_Poll) released on 27 March 2024, 55% of the responders in the United States disapproved of Israel's military action in Gaza, 36% approved, and 9% had no opinion.[^cite_note-Gallup-835] A poll from Generation Lab of 1,250 US college students conducted from 3–6 May reported that 8% had participated in a protest for either side.[^cite_note-836]

### Opinions of Generation Z in the Western world

The views espoused by members of [Generation Z](4015968.md) in the Western world in response to the war gained the attention of researchers and political analysts, as there is a [generational divide](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Generation_gap) between Generation Z and older generations on topics such as the [Israeli–Palestinian conflict](46216.md), [Hamas](13913.md), and the [American government's support for Israel](65284610.md).[^cite_note-\:03-837][^cite_note-838] Notably, members of Generation Z were more likely to support Palestine and to oppose Israel than any other generation.[^cite_note-brookings.edu-824] According to an October 2023 NPR/PBS NewsHour/Marist National poll which surveyed 1,313 U.S. adults, 48% of [Millennials](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Millennials) and Generation Z said that the U.S. government should publicly voice support for Israel, compared to 63% of [Generation X](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Generation_X), 83% of [Baby boomers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Baby_boomers), and 86% from the [Silent](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Silent_Generation) and [Greatest Generation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Greatest_Generation).[^cite_note-brookings.edu-824][^cite_note-Montanaro-825]

Between 13 and 14 December 2023, an online survey was conducted by [Harvard University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Harvard_University) and [The Harris Poll](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Harris_Poll) among 2,034 Americans from all age groups. While the survey assessed a range of political issues, there were several questions about the Gaza war. While the Harvard-Harris poll found that 73% of those aged 18–24 believed that the [7 October attack](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_Hamas-led_attack_on_Israel) was a terrorist attack and 66% of those aged 18–24 believed that the 7 October attack was genocidal in nature, 60% of those polled aged 18–24 believed that the 7 October 2023 attack was justified by Palestinian grievances, with smaller subsets of 50% stating that they sided with Hamas and 51% favoring a [one-state solution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/One-state_solution) where Israel would be ended and the land given completely to Hamas and the Palestinians. Furthermore, 67% of Generation Z respondents considered Jews to be oppressors, yet 69% of Generation Z respondents also stated that Israel has a right to exist.[^cite_note-\:03-837] Another poll in December 2023 was conducted by More in Common which surveyed various age groups, including Generation Z When members of Generation Z were asked whether they considered Hamas to be a terrorist organisation, Generation Z respondents were the most likely to consider Hamas to be freedom fighters and the least likely to consider Hamas a terrorist organisation.[^cite_note-\:17-839]

Some research has credited the stance of Western countries' members of Generation Z to [TikTok](56822861.md) and social media trends on the platform, with [*Axios*](52436164.md) noting that the platform has a large amount of Palestinian content on the website. The site found that 87% of TikTok users who view #StandwithPalestine posts are under the age of 35. In comparison, only 66% of TikTok users who view #StandwithIsrael posts are under the age of 35.[^cite_note-\:22-840] A trend was observed of Generation Z TikTok users sharing their [conversion to Islam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Conversion_to_Islam) following the Israel-Hamas war.[^cite_note-841] Lorenzo Vidino, director of the Programme on Extremism at [George Washington University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/George_Washington_University) has attributed this trend to a desire among Generation Z to rebel against Western values. Vidino stated: "At this point, what's more rebellious, what's more [anti-Western](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anti-Western_sentiment) and [anti-capitalism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anti-capitalism) and [anti-establishment](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anti-establishment), than a conversion to Islam?"[^cite_note-\:42-842]

## Analysis

Hugh Lovatt, a senior policy fellow with the Middle East and North Africa Programme at the [European Council on Foreign Relations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/European_Council_on_Foreign_Relations), analysed in January 2024, that the strong diplomatic and military support from much of the [Western world](21208200.md) (including US, UK, Germany) to Israel is "at odds with the attitudes of Western publics which continue to shift away from Israel". He said that during the [Cold War](325329.md), Israel sided with the West against the Arab countries supported by the Soviets, and Western leaders generally see Israel "as a fellow member of the [liberal democratic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Liberal_democracy) club", which partially "explains the continued strong Western support for Israel – which has now largely become reflexive".[^cite_note-Busari-2024-843] According to [Andrew Stroehlein](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Andrew_Stroehlein), writing for [Human Rights Watch](46530.md) in October 2024, most countries of the world have supported their own side of the conflict, without caring for [human rights](13831.md).[^cite_note-844]
